# 06 — Prioritized Product Backlog

**Product:** Multi-facility self-storage business application
**Status:** v1.0 — 2026-07-30
**Sources:** `00-master-prd.md`, `01-customer-website-prd.md`, `02-admin-dashboard-prd.md`, `03-hardware-integrations-prd.md`, `04-marketing-seo-prd.md`, `05-communications-prd.md`

This is the single, strictly-ordered build backlog for the whole platform. Items are sequenced so that every item is buildable when reached (all dependencies appear earlier), foundation comes first, and the shortest path runs to the two golden paths: **(1) a renter completes an online move-in with payment and receives a simulated gate code** (end of Milestone 2) and **(2) the billing engine invoices tenants and the comms module sends payment/past-due reminders** (end of Milestone 4). Numbering is global and continuous across milestones.

Sizes: **S** ≈ one short session, **M** ≈ one focused session, **L** ≈ 2–3 sessions or one long one, **XL** ≈ should be split during sprint planning. Phase = the phase the feature belongs to per the PRDs (master PRD wins on conflicts — see Flagged gaps/conflicts). A ✅ on the ID means the item is built and committed.

**Numbering.** B-numbers are global, continuous and permanent — never renumbered, never reused. The **#** column is positional. Rows added after the original sequencing carry a letter (`16a` = inserted between 16 and 17) so that inserting an item does not renumber seventy rows below it. The same rule applies recursively where a lettered position is itself full: `81da` = inserted after `81d`, before `81e`. B-093 onward were added on 2026-07-31 from the operator, UX and accessibility reviews; B-109–B-119 on 2026-08-12 from the second UX and accessibility reviews (of everything shipped through B-105); B-120–B-123 on 2026-08-14, out of building B-110 and B-112 and out of reviewing what the promotions engine actually reaches, rather than out of a review; B-124 and B-125 on 2026-08-14 out of building B-114; B-126 on 2026-08-14 out of building B-118; B-128 on 2026-08-17 out of B-082 part 6's own duplicate-content report; B-129 on 2026-08-18, split out of B-083 rather than raised — it is half of US-30, blocked on an open question older than the row it came from; B-130 on 2026-08-18, found by B-083's own e2e when no demo lease could generate a notice to send; B-131 on 2026-08-18, found while building B-084 part 1 — the occupancy report's date range does not apply to the occupancy figure; B-132 on 2026-08-18, found while fixing B-130 — three demo leases carry a delinquency label with no money behind it. B-133 on 2026-08-20, split out of B-087 rather than raised — it is the half of that row that needs Google API access and a policy read, and it is blocked in the same way B-129 and B-085 are. B-134 on 2026-08-20, raised by B-089's own measurement rather than by a review — D-77's gate correctly refuses the size pages a real portfolio would most want, and nothing in the product can currently unblock them. B-090a and B-135 on 2026-08-20 out of splitting B-090: the first is that row's part 1, the second is a defect its two-way-inbox evaluation turned up. B-090b on 2026-08-20 is that row's part 2, and B-136 on 2026-08-20 is a defect part 2 found in its own quote — the figure the tenant agrees to is not the figure the commit re-reads. **B-137–B-156 on 2026-08-21** from the third review block (operator, UX and accessibility, over B-120–B-136), inserted after B-130 so the whole block precedes every row that was open when it was written; each row states whether its finding was verified against the code or still needs confirmation at build time. Each row names the reviewer, or the item, that raised it. **B-157 on 2026-08-21** was raised while building B-137, not by a review: D-85 settled on Option A, B-137 built only the half every option agreed on, and the staff-side approval, reason code and unreset lien clock that Option A actually chose are still unbuilt. **B-158 on 2026-08-22** was diagnosed while building B-146, from a test failure the previous block's reason field made attributable. **B-159–B-184 on 2026-08-24** from the fourth review block (operator, UX and accessibility, over B-137–B-158), inserted after B-158 so the whole block again precedes every row that was open when it was written; positions continue the lettering under `83bv` (`83bva`–`83bvz`) so nothing below renumbers. Twenty-six rows from twenty-eight findings: near-identical findings were merged rather than numbered, and the merges are named in note 13. **B-185 on 2026-08-24** was measured while building B-161 rather than reviewed — the unit test database had accumulated 13,106 facilities and the sweep's failures had stopped being about the code; position `83bwa` continues the lettering past `83bvz`. **B-186 on 2026-08-25** was found while building B-174 — every walk-in tenant read as having given no notice at all; position `83bwb`. **B-187–B-196 on 2026-08-25** from the fifth review block (operator, UX and accessibility, over **B-159–B-184 plus B-185, B-186 and B-090c**), at positions `83bwc`–`83bwl`. Ten rows from thirty findings across three reports: the merges, the declines and the ordering are in note 14. **This is the first block whose rows depend on a row positioned BELOW them** — seven of the ten depend on **B-090c at `90e`** — and that is stated here rather than left to be discovered. The positional rule exists to guarantee an item is buildable when reached; B-090c is ✅ built, so it is, and putting ten confirmed defects behind the five partner-blocked rows at `83a`/`85`/`86`/`87a`/`89a` would stop the frontier instead. The exception is visible, reasoned and does not generalise: a dependency below its dependent is permissible **only** while that dependency is already ✅. **B-197 on 2026-08-27**, found while building B-190 rather than by a review: the four per-role monetary limits are seeded and edited by nothing, which B-004's own scope claimed and PRD 02 RBAC-2 asserts is not the case. **B-198 on 2026-08-27**, found while building B-191 at position `83bwn`: every template in the product ships text-only and CN-16's editor nulls the HTML body on every save, so a seeded HTML body — the only way to meet the one FR-9a criterion B-191 could not — would be silently erased by the first staff edit. **B-199 on 2026-08-27**, found while building B-192 at position `83bwo`: six e2e tests have failed on the phone viewport since B-090c and four consecutive items recorded them as "not this row's regression" without anyone raising the defect underneath — the tenant profile's seven-column leases table has no horizontal-scroll wrapper, so its action links sit outside a 375px document. **B-202–B-217 on 2026-08-29** from the sixth review block (operator, UX and accessibility, over **B-187–B-201 plus the B-129 partial**), at positions `83bwr`–`83bxf`. Sixteen rows from thirty-one findings across three reports; the merges are named on each row. **B-202 is first in the block and ahead of every other row here**, for the same reason B-137 led the third block: it is the only finding that can cost an operator a wrongful-sale claim rather than money, and its fix is one array element. **B-215 is deliberately last**, as B-119, B-156 and B-196 were before it, because it asserts what B-210 fixes. **B-208's remedy is an owner number and is blocked on a D-number**, not on code. Four honest disclaimers are not a fix. **B-224–B-243 on 2026-09-01** from the seventh review block, at positions `83bxm`–`83byf`. Those twenty rows are the **operator and digital-experience reports, over B-202–B-220**, and the accessibility criteria on them come from the criteria the affected screens already carry in the PRDs; **the accessibility pass over the same range reported late, after these twenty were committed, and is B-244–B-251 below**. Twenty rows from twenty-six findings across the two reports. **B-224 leads the block**, as B-202 and B-137 led theirs, and for the same reason: it is the only finding that costs a wrongful-sale claim rather than money — a sale schedulable before the served notice's deadline and recordable with no advertisement on file, which are the two commonest claims of that class — and its fix is two blockers and two refusals in the shape the pipeline already uses. **B-225–B-228 are the money-accuracy cluster and come next**, because four screens currently disagree about what one tenant pays: one of them is the autopay authorisation (B-227), one takes the money a second time from a tenant who prepaid (B-225), one advertises a discount it leaves out of its own total (B-226), and one names two different due dates for one installment (B-228). **B-229 sits ahead of every counter and portal row** because it is the alarm that makes the silent failures below it observable at all, and **B-236 depends on it** rather than the reverse — an unfinished cron run and a failed job must not look alike. **B-243 is deliberately last**, the way B-215 and B-196 were, but for the opposite reason: it may never be buildable, since D-63 forbids a simulator and the mail provider's status API is unconfirmed, so nothing behind it should be made to wait on it. **Six findings were merged rather than numbered**, and each merge is named on its row: the former-tenant AR that cannot be collected went into **B-231** with the POS screen that shows no balance (one screen, one picker); the walk-in rental recorded as a web rental went into **B-230** with the counter tender step (one code path, `startWalkInMoveInAction` → `provisionMoveIn`); the payment step's unbranched autopay disclosure went into **B-227** with the untaxed recurring figure (one component, one number); the billing-runs screen rendering job identifiers and cuids went into **B-229** with the job failure that alarms nobody, because an alarm nobody can read is not one; the un-itemised pay screen went into **B-232** with the gate-restore threshold (same two portal files, same tenant question); and the portal's missing Pay link went into **B-239** with the move-in confirmation that never mentions the account (one tenant, two consecutive screens, one missing route to paying). The UX report's dead `/admin/[section]` string — "built in a later backlog item", on a staff-facing route — was swept into **B-229** rather than given a row. **Five things were declined or dropped and none silently.** Tenant-initiated payment-plan requests stay declined exactly as in the B-187–B-196 block — new scope needing a policy the owner has not set, carried in PRD 01 §9 as an open gap — and only the one clause of past-due banner copy it identified is taken, inside B-232. The operator's **do-not-build list is left as it stands rather than acted on**: B-129 stays blocked rather than closed (closing a row is the owner's call, and D-104 already shipped what an operator actually needs), B-134 waits for a real portfolio to trip D-77's gate, and B-085's kiosk half is D-3's settled "Phase 3, default no". The `<details>`-based portal and admin menus that lose the reader's place on navigation were **left unresolved when these twenty rows were written**, because the UX reviewer handed them to an accessibility reviewer who had not yet reported; **that pass has now reported and refused them as a WCAG failure** — see B-244–B-251 below and the `aria-current` defect folded into B-239. The tenant profile's section **reorder** was not written, because it reverses **D-95**, which weighed and refused that exact trade — B-240 builds the anchor list and the sticky summary, which need no reversal, and the reversal is raised as **D-114**. A **pay link surviving move-out** was not written either, because it reverses **D-30**, and is raised as **D-112**. **Five questions are the owner's and are appended to `07-decisions.md`**: whether `reports:financial` is meant to gate the balance figure the counter needs (**D-110**, blocking B-231), whether `Tenant.email` stops being required and unique (**D-111**, blocking B-238), the D-30 amendment above (**D-112**), what a credit balance may do and what becomes of it at move-out (**D-113**, blocking B-225's disposition branch only), and the D-95 amendment above (**D-114**). **One dependency-below-dependent exception, and it is the fifth block's, unchanged**: B-228 depends on **B-090c at `90e`**, which sits below it — permissible only because B-090c is ✅ built, so the item is buildable when reached. Every other dependency in the block is either ✅ or positioned above its dependent. **B-244–B-251 on 2026-09-01 are the same block's accessibility rows, and they arrived late.** The pass ran over the same **B-202–B-220** range but reported *after* the first twenty rows were committed (`72211b1`), so it lands as a contiguous sub-block at positions `83byg`–`83byn` rather than interleaved by severity, and the passage above that said this block had no accessibility report has been corrected rather than left standing. Eight rows from nine findings. **B-243's "deliberately last" therefore means last of the first twenty** — nothing in the accessibility sub-block depends on it, so nothing here waits on a row that may never be buildable. **B-244 leads the sub-block**, on the same ground B-224 and B-202 led theirs: it is a verified **SC 1.3.1 (A)** failure on the customer money path, where a two-unit tenant's balance, late plan installment and Pay button are all announced under the other unit's heading. It sits behind the operator and UX rows only because it was written a commit later; **moving it ahead of them is a one-line position change and nothing depends on it either way — that call is the owner's.** **Every row states its success criterion and its level, and whether the finding is verified against the code or needs confirmation at build time, copied from the reviewer and never upgraded**; three rows carry an explicit split between the two (**B-245**, **B-246**, **B-251** — mechanism verified, behaviour or rendering unwatched). **Two rows carry a precision the reviewer was deliberate about, and it must survive any later edit.** **B-247 is a PRD 01 §6.2 shipping-gate miss, NOT a WCAG 2.1 AA failure** — 2.5.5 Target Size is AAA in WCAG 2.1 and 2.5.8 is WCAG 2.2 — and **B-249 carries the reviewer's own hedge**: whether a scroll container is a "user interface component" under 4.1.2 is genuinely contested, so it is written as arguable rather than as a clean A failure, because the reviewer declined to inflate it. What is not arguable on B-249, and is what the row is actually about, is that fifty-six focus stops announce nothing. **One finding was merged into an existing row rather than numbered**: finding 7's real defect — **no `aria-current` on any of the portal nav's eleven links** — went into **B-239**, which already rewrites that file's nav; it is **beyond AA (2.4.8 Location is AAA)** and the row says so. **The reviewer merged three live-region instances into one finding**, which is **B-245**. **The conformance call the digital-experience reviewer handed across was REFUSED, and the refusal is a finding in its own right**: the `<details>` menus closing on navigation are **not** a WCAG 2.1 AA failure — 3.2.3 Consistent Navigation (AA) is met because a disclosure resetting to its default state is a state and not a reordering, 4.1.2 (A) is met because native `<summary>` exposes `button` with expanded/collapsed, 2.4.3 concerns focus after navigation and is the router's business, and 2.4.8 Location is AAA — so **nothing is built for it**, and the real defect beside it is the `aria-current` gap now in B-239. A criterion refused by the reviewer who owns it is recorded here so it is not re-raised by the next pass. **Five rows already written in this block were amended IN PLACE rather than duplicated** — the B-085 pattern — with the criteria the reviewer supplied for each: **B-237** (3.3.4 AA satisfied by reusing the shipped `/admin/settings` confirm-and-echo, every control through `Field`/`FieldSet`, the new route joining `ADMIN_SCAN_ROUTES` in the same commit), **B-238** (**SC 3.3.2 A** — the field's hint names what the tenant loses without an email), **B-240** (the sticky summary and anchor list, the block's two highest-risk shapes, which carried no accessibility criteria at all: reflow at 320px and 200%, a real `<nav>` of links, focus moved to the target heading rather than scroll-only, a `<dl>` and not a live region), **B-241** (the false `<nav aria-label="Financial reports">` is **SC 4.1.2 A** and **SC 2.4.6 AA**, stated so it cannot be descoped as polish) and **B-242** (`alt=""` on decorative photos, the shipped two-span `×`, price-and-size as one accessible sentence). **B-239** was amended too, for the merged finding 7 above. **The four test-coverage gaps are on rows, not in prose, and each fold is named on the row that carries it**: the `layout:` field on `ScannedState` plus the coverage assertion, and opening every disclosure before `measureThreeWays`, into **B-246** (gap 1 *is* that finding's mechanism, and gap 4 is the same closed disclosure); the two-lease plan tenant in the demo seed into **B-244**; the client-side-navigation spec into **B-245**. None of the four is a defect on its own — each is the test that would have caught the finding it sits with, which is why none took a separate row. **Five things the reviewer declined are recorded rather than dropped**: **3.3.4 on the payment-plan builder** (conformant — `validateSchedule` meets the *Checked* satisfier by refusing cent-inexact and out-of-order schedules against the installment that caused each, with entries preserved by `AdminForm`'s `rememberSubmission`; a confirm step would be defensible product work and is not a conformance finding); **admin control heights at ~36px** (2.5.5 is AAA and 2.5.8 is WCAG 2.2, and §6.2's 44px rule is scoped to the customer site, which `form.tsx:471-482` already implements correctly); **`role="note"` on the auctions warnings and plan cards** (valid ARIA, poorly supported, harmless); **the lot-sheet CSV's link text** (2.4.4 Link Purpose is *In Context* at A/AA and the surrounding paragraph names the format); and **the contrast tokens** (`--muted-foreground` 4.73:1 light / 7.6:1 dark, the B-217 scrollbar thumb–track pair 4.34:1 light / 5.8:1 dark, all covered by `tests/contrast-tokens.test.ts`). `/admin/reports/revenue`'s `scope="rowgroup"` was raised and is already **B-222**. **One question is the owner's and is appended to `07-decisions.md`**: what moves `LAST_REVIEWED` on the accessibility statement, and how often the "Where we fall short" list is re-verified (**D-115**), which blocks **B-250's cadence half only** — the missing B-218 comment-log entry that row also carries needs no decision and is buildable today.

## Milestone 2: First online move-in (Golden Path 1)

Search → facility page → unit → reserve or rent → e-sign → Stripe payment → provisioned lease → simulated gate code → confirmation email.

| 79a | B-108 ✅ | **Staff MFA enrolment and sign-in: QR code, a save path for the recovery codes, and the second-factor field on a bare `/login`.** (1) `/mfa` shows only a 32-character base32 key to type by hand. The `otpauth://` URI it needs already exists and is already offered as a link — but that link only helps when the enrolling device *is* the phone, and the ordinary case is a key on a laptop screen and an authenticator in a pocket. Render the same URI as a QR, generated server-side and inlined (data URI or inline SVG) — the QR encodes the shared secret, so it must never be a fetchable URL, never be logged, and must die with the pending enrolment. **The typed key stays visible beside it, not behind a toggle**, and the accessibility review of 2026-08-12 corrected three things about how this row stated that: the criterion is **1.1.1 Non-text Content, Level A** — a stricter obligation than the AA this row claimed, and this row gets quoted in the accessibility statement; the image therefore takes **`alt=""`** because the adjacent key IS the text equivalent (`alt="QR code"` announces information it does not carry, and `alt={otpauthUri}` puts the shared secret into the accessibility tree, into AT logs and into extension dumps — the exact surface the rest of this row keeps it off); and the typed key is not yet an adequate equivalent, because `formatSecretForDisplay` groups base32 into pronounceable four-character blocks that VoiceOver reads as words, and `I`/`1`, `O`/`0`, `S`/`5` are indistinguishable by ear — it gains a character-separated `sr-only` reading matching `gate-code-panel.tsx`. Without that, the QR helps sighted staff and nobody else, inverting the item's own justification. (2) **Recovery codes get a way to save them** (UX review, finding 15): the copy says "this is the only time they are shown" and the screen offers no copy-all, no download, no print and no acknowledgement gate, while the codes live in a client component's `useActionState` — a refresh, a Back or a stray click loses them permanently, and the recovery path from there is an administrator reset on a product with one owner account. Copy-all and download-as-text beside the list with "Copied" announced from a persistent region (the `GateCodePanel` pattern), plus an "I have saved these codes" checkbox that must be ticked before the page will navigate on; identical treatment on regenerate; codes never logged. (3) **The sign-in form still infers the audience from `?from=`** even though D-47 fixed the lookup: the second-factor field renders only at `/login?from=/admin`, so an enrolled staff member reaching a bare `/login` submits without a code and is refused — the same "correct password rejected" symptom D-47 exists to kill. The field must be reachable for a staff account arriving with no hint, **without the form differing by whether an address exists** (no enumeration; the two-step "email, then code" shape is one way, always rendering it with its existing "leave blank if you have not set up two-factor authentication yet" hint is another — the item picks one and says which). In the same pass, the magic-link disclosure states as a general fact that staff sign in with a password and a code (D-40), since it is offered at a bare `/login` today and a staff member who uses it is told a link is on its way that flows.ts will never mint — a general sentence leaks nothing about any particular address | PRD 00 §7.1; PRD 01 §6.8.1 (portal dashboard, gate code); D-40, D-47 | M | B-079 | Phase 2 |

| 81dl | B-120 ✅ | **The e2e suite is not repeatable, and it does not notice when it is testing the wrong application** (found while building B-110). Three problems, one theme: a run that goes red tells you nothing until you have ruled the environment out by hand. (1) **A second full sweep fails without a reseed.** `admin-move-out`'s settlement preview mutates a seeded lease, `admin-tasks`' returned-mail flag finds the task it created last time, and the reservation specs exhaust the sandbox's inventory — each passes in isolation, so the failure reads as flakiness and is not. `global-setup.ts` already releases stale checkout locks and reservation holds and explains at length why that cost three diagnoses in one evening; the precedent is right and the coverage is short. Either specs provision and clean their own fixtures, or setup resets the ones they consume — pick one and say which, because doing half of each is how it got here. (2) **`baseURL` is hardcoded to port 3000 while `webServer` silently falls back to another port when 3000 is taken**, so a stray dev server from any other project makes the entire suite drive an application that is not this one. What that looks like is 300 tests failing with `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` and `net::ERR_EMPTY_RESPONSE`, which reads exactly like a code regression; it happened during B-110 and cost most of an hour. Fail on the port being occupied, or read the port back from the started server — never both hardcode and fall back. (3) **The e2e database is not covered by `db:migrate:test`.** That script migrates the `storage_test` SCHEMA, which is what `vitest.config.ts` redirects to; Playwright reads `DATABASE_URL` straight and wants `public` in the same database, so a fresh machine gets `The table public.checkout_session does not exist` from global setup before a single test runs. Needs its own named script beside the others and a line in CLAUDE.md, since the existing one is worded as though it covers everything | PRD 01 §6.8; the e2e suite itself | M | — | MVP |

Every promotional message the product can send goes by email. `comms/service.ts` says it plainly — B-072 shipped `marketing_email` only, there is no `marketing_sms` capture flow, so no marketing SMS rule is seeded and none could be evaluated if it were. | 81dp | B-124 ✅ | **A validation error must not discard what was typed** (found 2026-08-14 while building B-114). **Cause, once found: React 19 resets a `<form action>` once its action resolves** — no code in this repo clears anything, and the echo-into-`defaultValue` fix this row first prescribed could not have worked, because the inputs never remount. Every server action in the product therefore re-rendered its form empty after a field error. On the lease step that is not an inconvenience, it is a trap: mistype your signature and the re-render silently clears the **e-sign consent tick and the typed name**, so the next press is refused with "Tick the box to agree to sign electronically" for a box the renter did tick, on the last screen before payment. `activeDutyMilitary`, `altContactName` and `altContactPhone` clear alongside it. B-094 got the error *reporting* right (`aria-invalid`, `aria-describedby`, focused summary) and value *retention* was never part of it. 3.3.3 is not satisfied by an error that names a field the form just emptied. Fixed in `AdminForm`, which writes the submitted values back into the form's own controls on the error and confirm branches — one place, every control, no change to any of the 156 actions that return `fieldError`, and at the form rather than in `Field` because the protection step's radios are raw `<input>`s. Passwords, hidden fields, file inputs and buttons are excluded; restoring the confirm step's named button would have disarmed it. Consent stays stored only on success; the echo is a UI convenience, never a record of consent | PRD 02 §5.5 FR-19/FR-20; PRD 01 §6.8.1; WCAG 3.3.3 | M | B-094 | MVP |

| 81ds | B-127 ✅ | **Two friends redeeming the same referral invite in the same instant DEADLOCK instead of one losing** (found 2026-08-17 during B-082 part 4, in a full-suite run under parallel load). `qualifyReferral` opens a transaction that INSERTs a `referral` row — which takes a shared FK lock on the `referral_invite` it points at — and only THEN `UPDATE`s that same invite to claim it. Two concurrent qualifications each hold the shared lock and each wait for the other to release it before the update: Postgres reports `40P01 deadlock detected` and **both transactions roll back**, so neither friend gets the invite and one of the two rentals loses a $50 reward with no record of why. The race is already the tested case — `gives one invite to exactly one friend under a race` — and it passes because the losing path is exercised far more often than the deadlocking interleaving; it failed once in four full-suite runs and zero times in three isolated ones, which reads as flakiness and is not. **The fix is an ordering change, not a lock:** claim the invite FIRST (`updateMany ... WHERE redeemedAt IS NULL`), then create the referral, so the loser blocks on a row lock and finds `count === 0` rather than racing for the same lock from the other side. The refusal record the current code writes has to be preserved — the tenant is told which rule refused them (§6.3) — so the loser still writes a `refused` referral, just after losing rather than before. **Fixed during B-082 part 5, 2026-08-17, having been deferred one part earlier.** The judgement changed when it reproduced a second time: at roughly one full-suite run in three it would redden CI at random and made it impossible to report any suite honestly green, so the deferral was costing more than the fix. Claim first, create second — both transactions now take the same locks in the same order, which makes the deadlock impossible rather than unlikely. The loser is created `refused` outright instead of created `earned` and downgraded a statement later, removing a moment where a $50 referral was visible inside the transaction. **The race test was strengthened from two contenders to four**: at two it caught this bug about one run in three and never in isolation, and a guard that passes two times in three is not a guard. 0 failures in 7 full-suite runs and 12 isolated ones after the fix, against 2 in 6 before | PRD 10 §6.1; FR-REF-4 | S | B-100 | Phase 2 |

That is the check working, not a defect in it. The decision is the owner's: build an editable per-city description (a `City` model, an admin screen and a permission, per the rule that a field configuring behaviour ships with its control), or record that generated intros are accepted permanently and exclude generated-vs-generated pairs from the report so it stops reporting a state nobody intends to change. **Do not simply raise the threshold** — that hides the same finding for authored copy too, which is the case the check exists for. Until this is decided, the report's guidance for these pairs deliberately says "there is no city description to edit today" rather than telling somebody to rewrite a field that does not exist. **Decided 2026-08-17 (owner) — D-62: build the editable copy, keeping the generated intro as the fallback.** A `City` row holds authored prose and nothing else, keyed on the URL slug so it can never become a second opinion about where a city is or how it is spelled; `cityIntro` takes it and wins when it is there, so a city nobody has written for renders exactly what it rendered before — which is every city on the day this shipped. `/admin/settings/marketing/cities` lists every city that HAS a page (the list comes from the facilities, so copy cannot be published about a place we do not operate in), shows what each page says today rather than an empty box and a hint, and clearing the box is a real way back to generated. The permission is new — `marketing:city_copy`, asked for with a **null facility** like `org:defaults`, because a city page lists every site in the city and `facility:settings` at one of them would let a manager edit the page listing their neighbours'. **The duplicate-content report reads which one actually rendered**, so writing copy for a city moves it from `generated` to `authored` — and two authored cities that are still alike are now reported as a real copy problem instead of a product gap. Authored collisions sort above generated ones whatever the score. **It immediately caught B-082 part 2's own output**: the three demo city intros score **0.82–0.85** against each other, over this codebase's own 0.8 threshold, so by our own measure the city pages are duplicate content. Building a driver for it now would encode an answer to OQ-9 by building for it. **Built:** `mailNoticeCertified` posts the stored notice document by USPS certified mail through Lob's documented `POST /v1/letters`, hand-rolled with `fetch` (no SDK, the same call B-082 part 5 made for Search Console), and records the returned tracking number as proof of service. **No simulator, and this is the strongest case in the repo for that rule** — a simulated gate is a device we control (D-4) and a simulated index verdict was already refused in B-082 part 5, but a fabricated tracking number is a claim about what the POSTAL SERVICE did, sitting in the evidence chain that defends a lien sale under Texas Property Code ch. 59. Delivery is recorded through `recordNoticeDelivery` rather than by writing the `Notice` row, so B-061's consent and proof gates still run on this path. `deliveredAt` is the mailing date, because service by certified mail is complete on mailing — a refused or unclaimed letter does not undo it. **Found and fixed on the way:** both admin screens held two-entry notice-type label maps and printed the raw enum value (`late_notice`) for four of the six `NoticeType` members; hoisted to one total `noticeTypeLabel` | PRD 02 US-30; D-63 | M | B-061, B-062 | Phase 2 |

That last one is a seed defect, not the product working. Fix the seed so the delinquent lease's invoices and ledger agree, then add the e2e that generates a notice and serves it. **Fixed 2026-08-18. The cause was one missing field.** B-114 added the delinquent lease's INVOICE beside an already-existing ledger charge and never linked them, so the charge carried no `invoiceId` — and `reconcile` therefore counted the same $161 twice, once as an invoice outstanding and once as an uninvoiced charge, reporting the lease as short by exactly the amount owed. `generateInvoices` links every charge it raises; an unlinked one genuinely IS a charge no invoice accounts for, so the check was right and the seed was wrong. The invoice is now created first and the ledger entry carries its id. **A second refusal was behind it:** with the arithmetic fixed the lease refused with `no_template`, B-061's other deliberate rule, so the demo now seeds pre-lien AND lien templates for the primary facility — a demo of the lien arc that cannot produce its FIRST step demonstrates the end of a process without its beginning. | 83bv | B-158 ✅ | **A gate command can be skipped by clock skew between the app and the database** (diagnosed 2026-08-22 while building B-146, from the reason field B-137's test was given for exactly this purpose). `GateCommand.nextAttemptAt` defaults to Postgres `now()`; `drainGateCommands` selects `nextAttemptAt: { lte: now }` where `now` is a **Node-side `new Date()`**. The two clocks are independent — the database is remote — so a command inserted microseconds before a drain can carry a timestamp LATER than the drain's cutoff and be passed over. **This is not test-only.** `gate-simulator-db.test.ts`'s offline case is where it surfaces (intermittently, only under a full parallel sweep, passing in isolation — recorded as an unexplained failure on 2026-08-19 and diagnosed on 2026-08-22 when the assertion's `reason` came back **`unknown_code`**, which rules out the `outside_hours` wall-clock theory the comment had hedged on: the code never reached the simulated vendor at all). In production the same skip happens and self-heals on the next drain, which for a move-in gate code means **a tenant standing at a keypad that does not work** for however long until the next cron tick. The cheap fix is to stop comparing two clocks: drain on `nextAttemptAt <= NOW()` evaluated **in the database**, or default the column from the application clock that will later read it. Wants one test that a command inserted and drained in the same breath is always picked up | PRD 03 FR-3; PRD 03 US-6 | S | B-080 | Phase 2 |

The result of one returned ACH on a 90-day-old invoice: four dunning letters in the same post, late fees re-assessed on charges a superseded run already billed, access cut, and `engine.ts:353` `openAuctionCase` fired — the lien pipeline opened by a bank reversal, overnight, with no human in it. Builds: **at most one step per lease per run**; the ladder gated while an open `settling_payment_failed` task exists; resumption from the reached stage rather than from an empty history; and no re-assessment of a late fee a superseded run already charged. **POLICY — ANSWERED 2026-08-24 as D-92: ten days of grace, then RESUME at the stage already reached**, both per-facility configurable on the timeline (`reversalGraceDays` 0–90 default 10, `reversalResumes` default true) with the controls on the delinquency settings screen. Resume closes the pay-then-reverse loop that otherwise defers an auction indefinitely, and is defensible because every earlier notice really was served and is still in `DelinquencyStepRun`. | 83bvg | B-165 ✅ | **Batch ECRI raises every eligible tenant straight to full street rate, with no cap and no configurable rule** (operator review 2026-08-24; **verified**). `packages/core/pricing/rate-increase.ts:123-125` `targetRateFor` returns `candidate.streetRateCents` — that is the entire function — and `:89-92` `DEFAULT_ELIGIBILITY` (9 months, $15 gap) is a hardcoded module constant that `apps/web/app/admin/rate-increases/actions.ts:59-74` never overrides because no form field exists to override it with. D-88 settled that a bounce, a suppression hit or a missing send record blocks the increase and raises a task; `apps/web/app/admin/rate-increases/page.tsx:155-174` then renders help text and the shared cancel form on a `notice_failed` row and nothing else, and the copy says "Cancel it and schedule it again" — which means re-typing a Lease ID into B-153's free-text form (fixed by B-177) and re-deriving the delta by hand, per row, with no batch action for a bounced-domain event that held forty of them. | 83bvi | B-167 ✅ | **Six configurable fee types can be configured and cannot be charged, and no ad-hoc charge exists anywhere** (operator review 2026-08-24; **verified**). `packages/db/prisma/schema.prisma:411-422` defines `lock_cut`, `cleaning`, `damage`, `lien`, `certified_mail` and `auction_cost`, and `apps/web/app/admin/settings/page.tsx:50-65` gives every one of them an editable schedule — while repo-wide only `admin`, `late`, `transfer` and `nsf` are ever charged. `apps/web/app/admin/tenants/[tenantId]/actions.ts` exports thirteen actions and **not one of them posts a charge**. So a unit left full of rubbish, a lock cut at the operator's cost and a damaged door are all settings screens with no consequence, and the certified-mail and lien costs the operator is legally allowed to recover never appear in a cure quote — the tenant pays less than the statute permits and the operator eats the postage. The authority gate is measured against the **departure from the schedule in EITHER direction** against the existing fee-waiver limit: under-charging is a waiver by another name, over-charging is the direction that hurts the tenant, and one symmetric rule needs no new column — a counter staffer's $0 limit means "the facility's price, exactly", and an unpriced fee type is a departure from zero so all of it needs waiver authority. And it uncovered a **real, latent bug that had to be fixed first**: `(Invoice.leaseId, periodStart)` was unique across every invoice KIND, so a lease could carry only one fee invoice per calendar day — a move-out posting cleaning and damage on the same afternoon lost the second silently, and an NSF fee on a day that already had a late fee threw. Now a partial unique scoped to `kind: 'rent'`; late fees keep `chargedSteps`, which was always the real guard. Three copies of the fee-invoice shape became one primitive. **Leaves behind:** a fee on an ENDED lease is not autopay-collected (correctly — the settlement collects it); no approval queue for an over-limit override, same posture as `waiveFeeInvoice`; `auction_cost` still has no automatic poster; the recapture B-168 owns is still a raw ledger entry | PRD 02 §4.5 US-21/US-23, §4.4 US-14 (move-out), §4.1 US-3; D-10 | M | B-146, B-040, B-004 | MVP |

The only lever `CompleteMoveOutInput` (`move-out.ts:167-177`) offers is `writeOff?: boolean` — all or nothing across the **entire** residual, so forgiving a disputed $60 recapture also forgives $400 of genuine arrears, in one unaudited boolean. Builds: post the recapture as a `kind: 'fee'` invoice on the NSF shape B-146 established, so every existing tool reaches it; a reduce-or-waive override carrying a reason code and gated by `checkMonetaryAuthority` **on the amount forgiven**, not on the amount charged; the recapture rendered as its own settleable line on the final statement beside arrears rather than folded into a residual; and recapture-collected versus recapture-waived reportable per facility, which is the number that says whether the minimum stay is doing anything. Keeps D-89 intact — a transfer still recovers nothing. **Built as specified.** The reduction flows through the SETTLEMENT rather than being subtracted after it, so `amountDueCents`, the write-off threshold and the manager-override decision all read one number — subtracting afterwards would leave the screen demanding a manager for a debt that no longer exists. The override is **clamped to the ruled figure**: an operator may forgive a recapture, never invent one, and over-charging needs no reason because nothing was forgiven. `applyRecaptureOverride` rewrites the reason sentence to say what was forgiven and that sentence stays on the LEDGER row, keeping B-145's rule that the tenant's statement must not use different words from the screen that got their consent. | 83bvk | B-169 ✅ | **Nothing finds the overlocks already stuck, and the removal task tells staff the tenant has paid when they have not** (operator review 2026-08-24; **verified**; B-151's own named tail, plus a defect B-151 introduced). `packages/core/tasks/catalog.ts:254-257` still labels the task "Take the overlock off — the tenant has paid", unchanged by B-151, which now also raises `overlock_remove` after an auction sale and after an abandonment — so the card asserts a payment that did not happen, on the two paths where the tenant most certainly did not pay. | 83bvl | B-170 ✅ | **The task-completion form asks for two of the four proof fields, and announces nothing when it works** (UX **and** accessibility reviews independently, 2026-08-24; **verified**; SC 4.1.3 AA, 2.4.3 A, 3.3.1 A, 3.3.2 A). One screen, one session, three findings merged. (1) `components/admin/task-complete-form.tsx:38-53` renders `note` and conditionally `photo_reference` and nothing else, `apps/web/app/admin/tasks/actions.ts:24-36` forwards only those two, and `packages/core/delinquency/timeline.ts:52` `PROOF_FIELDS` also contains `tracking_number` and `delivered_on` — which `admin/settings/delinquency/page.tsx:238` lets an operator require. Such a task **can never be completed from that screen**, and the refusal is the raw enum key (`` `${field} is required.` ``). (2) `completeTaskAction` (`actions.ts:42`) returns `success('Task completed.')` and revalidates in the same commit, so React writes the message into `AdminForm`'s `role="status"` and unmounts the `<li>` containing it simultaneously — the text never exists long enough to be an observable mutation, and focus, which was on the submit inside that `<li>`, falls to `<body>` so the next Tab starts from the sidebar. The same pattern breaks "Make live" on `admin/settings/promotions/page.tsx:133-158` and "Record the return" on `admin/tenants/[tenantId]/page.tsx:923-984`, because in each the row reporting the outcome is the row the outcome removes. This is the failure B-148 fixed for the two public marketing forms and never reached the admin side. (3) The note's only visible label is the placeholder "What did you do?" (3.3.2). Builds: an input for **every** field in `task.requiredProofFields` driven from one label map, with human labels (`tracking_number` → "Tracking number", `delivered_on` → "Date delivered", `type="date"`) and a real `<label>` on each — no per-field booleans, and an unmapped key is a **build-time** failure rather than a raw key shown to staff; plus an opt-in `announceOutside` region rendered by the PAGE above the list, so the message survives the row it reports on — the structural move `components/marketing/form-result.tsx:48-50` already makes — with focus moved to it via `tabIndex={-1}`. The list heading is the natural target ("Tasks (11)" → "Tasks (10)"), which answers "did it work" with a countable fact. Acceptance: after a successful completion `expect(page.getByRole('status')).toContainText('Task completed')` **and** `expect(page.locator('body')).not.toBeFocused()`, asserted on all four queues that render this component (`admin/tasks`, `admin/delinquency`, `admin/walkthrough`, `admin/access/queue`) | PRD 02 §4.9 US-41, §5.5 FR-19/FR-20/FR-22/FR-24 | M | B-141, B-095, B-094 | MVP |

Nothing on any of the four screens says the picker is inert until a second button is pressed: `admin/tenants/[tenantId]/move-out/page.tsx:104-122` versus `:202` (`moveOutDate` read from `searchParams` at `:70-74`); `admin/tenants/[tenantId]/transfer/page.tsx:115-140` versus `:216`; `portal/move-out/page.tsx:155-174` versus `:234-238`; `portal/transfer/page.tsx:253-264` versus `:326`. Press Recalculate to see what a Sep 5 move-out settles to."), and the commit button restates the date it is about to act on. **The row offered the two fixes as alternatives and it needed both.** Moving the control into the committing form alone only mirrors the defect — it then commits the typed date against figures worked out for a different one, which is the same lie with the operands swapped. | 83bwa | B-185 ✅ | **The unit test database accumulates fixtures until the sweep stops being trustworthy** (found while building B-161, 2026-08-24; **measured**). `storage_test` held **13,106 facilities and 8,276 units** — every one of them created in August 2026, none by design. Only 32 of ~120 database suites call `facility.deleteMany`, so the rest leave their fixtures behind on every run, for ever. Two failures fall out of it and BOTH read as product bugs: `tests/marketplace-db.test.ts` calls `marketplaceFeed()`, which scans the whole facility table with unit counts, and under parallel load it holds a Prisma connection past the 10s pool timeout — four tests fail with *"Timed out fetching a new connection from the connection pool"*, and the same file passes 10/10 alone; and `admin-tenants-db.test.ts`'s `searchTenants` case fails because `searchTenants` caps at 25 results and hundreds of leftover "Ada Renter" rows now fill them. A clean-tree sweep on 2026-08-24 was **4 failed / 3,583 passed**; dropping and re-migrating the schema is what made it green again. Builds: a per-suite cleanup discipline that a new suite cannot silently skip (a shared `afterAll` helper keyed to the suite's own `suffix`, or a truncate-between-files hook), and — separately — whichever of the two failing assertions should be scoped to its own fixtures rather than to the whole table regardless. **The reset is a local command today and nobody is told to run it**, which is the part that makes this a row rather than a note: the failure looks exactly like a broken feed and a broken tenant search **A sibling mechanism, found while building B-180, 2026-08-25:** the full sweep failed once on `comms-observability-db.test.ts > alertOwner`, which expected its own owner and got another suite's (`owner-case-…@example.com`). `ownerEmail()` in `apps/web/lib/comms/alerts.ts` is a `findFirst` for **any** active owner with no ordering, so it resolves to whichever owner fixture happens to exist — and under a parallel run that is a **live race between concurrently running suites**, not accumulation: the suite passed alone, passed on the re-run, and the database held zero `owner-*` rows afterwards. Cleaning up after every suite does not fix this one; the suite has to be able to tell its own owner apart, or `alertOwner` has to be given one. **Built differently from the row's own first idea, because that idea turned out to be impossible.** A shared `afterAll` cleanup or a truncate-between-files hook both assume a suite CAN delete what it created; it often can't. `audit_log`'s append-only migration (20260730174727) gives it a RESTRICT foreign key to `facility` and `staff_user`, plus a statement-level trigger that raises on UPDATE, DELETE, *and* TRUNCATE against itself — "deliberately no in-band escape hatch," per that migration's own comment. A `TRUNCATE ... CASCADE` on `facility` forces Postgres to also truncate `audit_log` (CASCADE follows every incoming FK, RESTRICT notwithstanding), which the trigger then refuses — confirmed by building the truncate hook first and watching it fail with exactly that error. Any fixture a suite ever audit-logged against is permanently unwipeable by row-level cleanup, however disciplined the suite is; `access-service-db.test.ts` already carries a comment naming this ("Not the facility: revealCode's audit entries hold a Restrict FK to it") for exactly this reason. So per-suite cleanup can only ever reclaim what audit_log hasn't pinned, and no lint or shared helper changes that. **What shipped instead:** `npm run db:reset-test` (`scripts/reset-test-schema.mts`) — drops and rebuilds the `storage_test` schema in one command, which is what actually fixed the 2026-08-24 incident and was, until now, a manual recipe nobody was told about. Deliberately not wired into `npm test` itself, for the same "too slow to tax every run" reason `db:migrate:test` already isn't. **The ownerEmail race got its own fix, unrelated to the truncate question.** `alertOwner`/`ownerEmail` in `apps/web/lib/comms/alerts.ts` take an optional `Prisma.StaffUserWhereInput` (`ownerWhere`) narrowing which staff row counts as "the" owner; every production call site (three in `detectors.ts`, one in `jobs/registry.ts`) omits it and is unaffected. `comms-observability-db.test.ts` now scopes to its own fixture's email — built as a function re-evaluated per `it()`, not a `describe`-body const, because the const version froze on the empty string the `beforeAll` hadn't set yet when the `describe` callback ran during collection (caught by the test itself failing, not by inspection). **Left behind, and it should stay open rather than be quietly declared done:** the two originally-reported failing assertions (`marketplace-db.test.ts`'s `marketplaceFeed` connection-pool timeout, `admin-tenants-db.test.ts`'s `searchTenants` cap) were not independently patched — both were symptoms of the same accumulation `db:reset-test` now has a one-command fix for, not defects in the assertions themselves (the "Ada Renter" search there is already suffix-scoped and was, on inspection here). No enforcement exists yet to stop a new suite from leaving fixtures behind the way ~78 of 106 already do; building one that doesn't false-positive on the audit-pinned cases is real, undone work. | PRD 08; CLAUDE.md "Test isolation is not free on a shared database" | M | — | MVP |

| 83bwd | B-188 ✅ | **A payment plan counts money that came back, and money that was never meant for it** (operator review findings 1 and 2 merged with UX review finding 3, 2026-08-25; **verified**). Three defects, one function, and it is the function the whole feature rests on. `apps/web/lib/admin/payment-plans.ts:222-229` `paidSincePlanStart` sums `LedgerEntry` where `type: 'payment'` and `occurredAt >= plan.createdAt`, and that sum IS progress: `installmentViews` allocates it oldest-first, `isFullyPaid` closes the plan, `lib/delinquency/payment-plan-breach.ts:55-62` lifts the hold on the strength of it. **(1) A returned payment stays counted.** `lib/billing/reversals.ts:206-214` posts a reversal as a positive `type: "adjustment"` and deliberately leaves the original `payment` row alone — its own comment says why — and `lib/billing/refunds.ts:176` posts `type: 'refund'`. So a bounced ACH, a chargeback and a refund each leave every installment they covered reading `paid`; a plan can **complete itself and lift its own hold on money the bank took back**, which re-opens one layer down the pay-then-reverse loop D-92 closed one layer up. **(2) Ordinary rent satisfies the arrears.** Nothing filters on which invoice a payment settled, so a tenant on a plan for $1,800 of arrears who pays only next month's $150 rent has installment 1 marked `paid`, and dunning, late fees and access suspension all stay halted while the plan collects nothing and reports progress. **(3) The plan is never checked against what is owed.** `payment-plans.ts:62` computes `totalCents` as the sum of the installments and then hands both to `validateSchedule(installments, totalCents, now)`, whose `sum !== totalCents` branch (`packages/core/payment-plans/schedule.ts:61-66`) is therefore **unreachable from the only production caller** — a $50 plan against an $1,800 arrear validates, halts the ladder and completes. Builds: progress computed **net of reversals, chargebacks and refunds** from the same signed ledger rather than from `type: 'payment'` alone, with a plan whose progress goes backwards re-evaluated that night rather than at the next installment; the plan total validated against the lease's actual outstanding balance at creation, naming the shortfall in the refusal; and the dead branch made reachable or removed. **Keep the derivation** — no stored per-installment paid flag, per B-090c's own D-65 reasoning: this row changes what is summed, not where it lives. **POLICY, and this row cannot be finished until it produces a new D-number:** whether an installment is satisfied by ANY payment, or only by payment **in excess of the current month's rent**. That sentence decides whether a tenant on a plan owes rent-plus-installment or installment-only; it is the most consequential line in the plan copy and the one an operator is held to at the counter | PRD 02 §4.6 US-25; PRD 01 §9; D-25; D-65; D-92 | M | B-090c, B-161, B-147 | Phase 3 |
| 83bwe | B-189 ✅ | **Autopay does not know a payment plan exists, in either direction** (operator review finding 3, 2026-08-25; **verified**). `packages/core/holds/catalog.ts:122-129` gives the `payment_plan` hold the effects `halt_dunning`, `halt_late_fees` and `halt_access_suspension` — **not `halt_autopay`**, which is the only effect `apps/web/lib/billing/autopay.ts:150-165` checks. `autopay.ts:121-127` then selects every `open`/`partially_paid` invoice with `dueDate <= businessDate` and charges the **full outstanding**, on the retry ladder, for as long as the card authorises. So a tenant who agreed to clear $1,800 over six months has their card taken for $1,800 the same night the plan is agreed: the precise outcome the plan exists to prevent, executed by the system that was told about it, and the fastest possible route to the chargeback B-147 built the handling for. The other direction is the mirror — **nothing ever charges an installment.** An installment due date is a date on which no charge is initiated, so a tenant with a saved card must remember to pay by hand or the hour-4 breach job breaks their plan and resumes collections. Builds: the arrears half of autopay stands down for a lease on an active plan while **current rent keeps being collected** — a plan is forbearance on what is owed, not a rent holiday — and the installment is collected on its due date from the saved method where the tenant has one, through the existing ledger-driven `PaymentIntent` path. **Not Stripe Billing, not a subscription, not a schedule object (D-6):** the ledger stays the source of truth and an installment is one more due date it charges. A failed installment charge is a failed payment on US-20's retry ladder (CN-6), **not an immediate breach** — a decline and a decision not to pay are different facts and the breach job must not treat them alike. **POLICY, and this row cannot be finished until it produces a new D-number:** whether the installment is auto-collected at all, or whether a plan is deliberately manual-pay. B-167 settled that a departure from the fee schedule needs authority in EITHER direction; a plan is a decision not to collect, made by one person, on a lease already in collections, and it has less. **(2) No cap on the chain.** `payment-plans.ts:56-60` refuses a second plan only while one is `active`, so a plan broken last night is replaceable this morning, indefinitely — each new plan re-halts dunning, late fees and access suspension, and the lien clock never runs. | 83bwm | B-197 ✅ | **The four monetary limits that decide who may give money away are reachable only from a database client** (found while building B-190, 2026-08-27; **verified — `maxFeeWaiverCents` has three readers in `apps/web` and no writer anywhere**). `Role.maxFeeWaiverCents`, `maxRefundCents`, `maxCreditCents` and now `maxPlanDeferralCents` are seeded from `packages/db/rbac-catalog.ts` and edited by nothing. **B-004's own scope claimed this** — its row reads "monetary authority limits config" — and PRD 02 RBAC-2 says the limits "are starting values the owner can change in configuration; they are not hardcoded policy", which is currently false. Checking every field on every save means a ladder that was already crooked when the screen loaded (a seed edited by hand, a role inserted below an existing one) blocks an unrelated field, and the only way out is the database client this row exists to remove. **(2) Blank and `0` are opposite facts and the round trip has to keep them apart** — unlimited against no authority at all — so `parseScaled` is never reached for an empty box and nothing in the chain may `?? 0`. `describe()` is the one place that turns either into words, used by the table, the announcement and the audit message alike. **(3) Five forms, not one grid of inputs in the table.** A cell-level input would have to reach its `AdminForm` through the `form=` owner attribute, which carries the DOM but not the React context `Field` reads its error from — so 3.3.1 would have been met by a summary at the top of the page and nothing beside the field. The table stays read-only and says today's figures; the forms sit under it, one per role, each named after its role. **Left behind:** the ladder is checked on save and never re-checked afterwards, so adding a role BELOW an existing one with a bigger limit still produces a crooked chain that only the next edit of that action reports — a role editor is not built and B-004 still owns it | PRD 02 §3 Roles & Permissions, RBAC-2, §5.5; D-12; D-98 | S | B-004, B-190 | Phase 3 |

| 83bwq | B-201 ✅ | **No dynamic admin route has ever been reflow-checked, and `[contain:layout]` means the check that exists cannot see a missing scroll wrapper** (found while building B-199, 2026-08-28; **verified — `ADMIN_ROUTES` in `e2e/admin.spec.ts` is `ADMIN_SCAN_ROUTES`, and the seven routes in `SCANNED_BY_OWN_SPEC` appear in no reflow, zoom or text-spacing loop**). Two halves of one gap, both found by a defect the existing sweep did not catch. **(1) The 320px loop runs over the static route list only.** `SCANNED_BY_OWN_SPEC` exists because a dynamic route needs a live fixture to render its real content, and B-156 correctly made those routes axe-scanned by their own specs — but only axe. `/admin/tenants/[tenantId]`, its ledger, its notices, move-out and transfer, plus `/portal/transfer` and `/portal/pay`, have never been checked at 320px, at 200% zoom, or under forced text spacing. B-199's defect lived on one of them for four items. **(2) The document-level assertion is masked by design.** B-116 put `[contain:layout]` on `<main>` so a correctly-wrapped table's own scroll region would stop Chromium's root-level `scrollWidth` walk. It stops the walk either way: a table with **no** wrapper overflows inside a containing block the document cannot see past, so `documentElement.scrollWidth` stays at 320 and the assertion passes while the columns are unreachable by any means — which is strictly worse than the horizontal scroll the check exists to catch. **Seven tables across five admin screens were in exactly that state** and every one of those routes was passing its reflow test. Builds: the reflow, zoom and text-spacing loops reaching the own-spec routes through the same click-through their axe scan already uses (a shared helper, not seven copies); and a second assertion that does not go through the document — the cheapest honest one is that no element's right edge exceeds the viewport, or that every `overflow-x-auto` wrapper whose table overflows is a real scroll region, which is what B-199's own pin does for one page. **The `[contain:layout]` rule is not the bug and must not be removed** — B-116 measured it and its reasoning holds; what is missing is a check it cannot mask **Built 2026-08-28.** Both halves closed, and the second one paid for itself immediately: **seven real defects on four admin routes that had been green throughout**, none of them visible to the document-level assertion. `e2e/a11y-own-spec-routes.spec.ts` reaches all seven `SCANNED_BY_OWN_SPEC` routes through ONE table of click-throughs and measures each at 320px, at 200% zoom and under forced text spacing; a route in that list with no entry in the table is a failing test, so this cannot silently fall behind the axe coverage the way it already had. Every reflow/zoom/text-spacing loop in the suite — public, portal, admin and the new one — now routes through one `expectNoHorizontalOverflow`, which asks per ELEMENT whether anything is painted past the right edge of the screen that no scrollbar reaches. `[contain:layout]` is untouched, as the row required. **Six of the seven defects are one bug**: `Field`'s wrapper is a flex item, its `min-width: auto` holds it at the `<select>`'s longest option, and B-116's `max-w-full` on `CONTROL_CLASS` was therefore capping the control against a parent that was already too wide — the pair cancelled out, and B-116's own comment names one of the six controls it did not actually fix. `min-w-0` is PREPENDED to every `Field` wrapper rather than written into its default string, and that distinction is the finding: five files declare a local `FIELD_CLASS` that is a hand-copy of that default and REPLACES it, so the first fix reached `/admin/settings` and left `/admin/rate-increases` and the transfer wizard exactly as broken. The seventh is `GOOGLE_SEARCH_CONSOLE_CLIENT_EMAIL`, which has no break opportunity in it — `break-all`, the same fix as B-094's JSON example. **The first version of the check was unusable and is worth recording**: comparing `scrollWidth > clientWidth` per element produced 60+ findings across seven routes and not one was a defect — `sr-only` is `position: absolute`, so an sr-only span inside a `position: static` `overflow-x-auto` table is not clipped by it, escapes to the nearest positioned ancestor and single-handedly pushed `<main>`'s scrollWidth to 675px on a page that renders correctly; and Chromium lays out `content-visibility: hidden` subtrees, so the portal nav's CLOSED "Manage" menu reported six links overflowing a column nobody can see. Both exclusions are named in the helper with the reason. **Verified against a real failing run rather than assumed**: deleting the tenant profile's `overflow-x-auto` reproduces B-199's defect and the check names it — `table.w-full.min-w-2xl.text-sm — right edge at 696px, screen is 320px` — then goes green again when it is restored. Three identical copies of `TEXT_SPACING` were deleted for one | PRD 02 §5.5 FR-24 (WCAG 2.1 AA, 1.4.10 Reflow); B-116; B-156; B-199 | S | B-199 | MVP |
| 83a | B-129 | **Online auction platform listing from the auction pipeline** (split from B-083, 2026-08-18, D-63). (2) A StorageTreasures-class listing needs a **partner agreement**; no API is published without an account, so there is no spec to write against, which is exactly why B-085's gate-vendor driver is gated the same way. **The rule B-083 settled applies here too (D-63): no simulator.** A fabricated "listed on 3 August" record is a claim about a public advertisement that Texas requires before a sale, and an advertisement that never ran is the commonest wrongful-sale claim after an unserved notice. When it is unblocked, the cheap first move is a structured listing export (lot, photos, address, sale date, terms) an operator uploads themselves — it is useful whichever way OQ-9 goes and fabricates nothing **Partially built 2026-08-29 (owner, D-104): the lot sheet ships; the marketplace driver stays blocked and this row stays open for it.** Both blockers are still blockers — OQ-9 is unanswered and there is still no partner agreement — so what was built is exactly the move this row already named as safe: a structured lot sheet (`/admin/auctions/lots.csv`) with lot, facility address, unit size, sale date and terms, which an operator uploads to a marketplace, hands to an auctioneer or reads to a newspaper. Neutral between live and online, so **OQ-9 is not answered by building for it** — the thing D-63 refused. **No `AuctionAdvertisement` row is written by exporting**: downloading a sheet is not evidence an advertisement ran, and that row stays typed by whoever placed the ad. **The load-bearing half is the refusal list, not the download.** The row's own framing is that an advertisement that never ran is the commonest wrongful-sale claim after an unserved notice; the mirror image is one that RAN for a sale that could not lawfully happen. A lot goes up days ahead, and in that window the tenant may pay, an SCRA or bankruptcy hold may land, or somebody may record a titled vehicle — **each of which leaves `status` at `scheduled`**. So `selectListableLots` decides on live `auctionReadiness`, the same function the case screen refuses on, and never on the stored status; every dropped case is named on `/admin/auctions` with its blockers rather than being silently missing from a short file. **Terms of sale are a new `Facility.auctionSaleTerms` with its control shipped in the same item** (this repo's own rule), on the delinquency settings page rather than in the timeline form — saving the timeline mints a VERSION every governed lease stays pinned to, and editing a comma in the terms should not bump the version each approval screen cites. Null stays a real state: blank column, and the screen says so. **Photographs are not in the sheet and that is a real gap, not an oversight** — inventory photo references live inside the hashed evidence document `recordLockCut` renders, and there is no per-lot image store; re-keying them into a CSV would create a second copy of primary evidence that can disagree with the hashed original. **Still blocked:** the marketplace driver itself, on OQ-9 and a partner agreement, exactly as before | PRD 02 US-30; master PRD §11 OQ-9; D-63 | M | B-062, B-083 | Phase 2 |

| 83bxb | B-213 ✅ | **The builder's per-installment refusal reaches no assistive technology, and no test in the repo renders one** (accessibility review finding 1 and test gap 1, 2026-08-29; **verified**; SC 3.3.1 A, 3.3.3 AA; admin). `FieldSet` puts `aria-invalid` and `aria-describedby` on the `<fieldset>` itself (`apps/web/components/admin/form.tsx:390-407`), reasoning that both are global attributes. Allowed is not conveyed: no shipping screen reader announces a group's `aria-describedby` or its invalid state when focus lands on a control inside it, and `aria-invalid` has no mapping on `role=group` at all. The meaningless `aria-invalid` is off the fieldset; `aria-describedby` stays there for the hint, which has the same unannounced problem in a smaller way and is named in PROGRESS rather than fixed. The mechanism has three paths, not one: `HAND_CHECKED_INCOMPLETE` is genuinely route-keyed (`e2e/a11y-helpers.ts:79-116`) and the sentence is true of it; `VERIFIED_BY_HIT_TEST` (`:154-157`) waives two checks on **every route**, re-proving per node, which is defensible engineering but is not what the sentence says; and `if (n.target.length !== 1) return false` (`:233`) drops **every `incomplete` node inside any iframe, on every route, with no hand check** while the comment above it describes a Stripe-specific waiver. There is a second-order hole inside the hit test: `:173` waives when the element's centre is off-screen, which is exactly B-199's case — an element off-screen *unreachably* — leaving **1.4.3** and **1.4.11 (AA)** silently unenforced there. **Fix: correct the sentence to be true of all three paths; narrow `:173` to waive only when a horizontally scrollable ancestor exists, reusing `scrollsHorizontally` (`:345-351`); and scope or declare the iframe drop.** While on the page, replace the three-clause "everywhere they run" sentence (`:801-804`) with plain language that keeps every commitment. `LAST_REVIEWED` does not move — no claim changes. **Built 2026-08-31.** The sentence is now three bullets, one per path, because the mechanism has three and prose that averages them is what made it false. **Two of the three were narrowed as well as described, and neither narrowing can waive more than before.** (1) The hit test waived any node whose centre point is off-screen; it now returns `scrollsHorizontally(el)`, so the waiver holds only where a scrollbar brings the node back — B-199's case, painted past the edge and unreachable by any means, now fails 1.4.3 and 1.4.11 instead of being waived. The ancestor walk is **duplicated** from `offscreenOffenders` rather than shared, with a comment saying so on both copies: a closure cannot cross the `page.evaluate` boundary, and injecting the source through `new Function` is the kind of cleverness this repo decodes at 3am. (2) `if (n.target.length !== 1) return false` dropped every undecided node inside ANY iframe on every route; it is now scoped to a frame whose `src` is cross-origin, so Stripe's Payment Element and the Maps embed stay waived and a frame we author is checked like any other markup. Stripe keeps its name match too, because several of its frames carry no `src` and the origin test alone would start reporting markup we cannot fix. The iframe ELEMENT is ours wherever it points, so a single-target node on it is untouched. **The scan contract is unchanged in the other direction:** no route, state or exception list moves, and the waivers only got smaller. **Verified by a full 1310-test sweep in which every axe scan passed.** The sweep's five failures are all pre-existing and none is reachable from this diff — four are B-220 (raised here), one is B-221 (raised here, cause not identified) | PRD 01 §6.8; B-187; B-196 | M | B-196, B-201 | Phase 2 |
