| 83bxg | B-218 ✅ | **`gh pr ready` runs no e2e, so the heavy lane has never once gated a pull request** (found while marking B-206 ready, 2026-08-29; **verified** — `gh pr ready 6` succeeded and `gh run list` showed no run after it, and all sixteen of PR #1–#16 report `e2e=skipping`). `.github/workflows/ci.yml` declares `on: pull_request` with no `types:`, which defaults to `opened, synchronize, reopened` — **`ready_for_review` is not among them**. So clearing draft fires nothing, and the `e2e` job keeps the `skipping` conclusion its `github.event.pull_request.draft == false` guard gave it while the PR still was a draft. `gh run rerun` cannot recover it: a re-run replays the original event payload, where `draft: true`. The split lanes were built so ~30 minutes of Actions time would be spent on PRs actually up for review; the effect was that it was spent on none of them, and every e2e pass this repo has ever had came from a merge to `main` — i.e. **after** the point the gate exists to guard. **Fix: add `types: [opened, synchronize, reopened, ready_for_review]`.** Sixteen open PRs each need one more draft→ready toggle (or any push) once this is merged, because the fix reaches them through their merge ref rather than retroactively | `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; repo CLAUDE.md "Pull requests" | S | — | Phase 2 |
| 83bxh | B-219 | **The document store promises "newest first" and cannot keep it for two documents written in the same millisecond** (found while building B-206, 2026-08-30; **verified** — the assertion fails ~2 runs in 3 on `main`, unchanged by B-206). `documentsFor` (`apps/web/lib/documents/store.ts:82-87`) orders on `createdAt` alone, and `Document.createdAt` is `@default(now())`, which Prisma generates **client-side at millisecond precision** rather than letting Postgres fill the `TIMESTAMPTZ(6)` default the migration already declares (`20260801150000_document_store/migration.sql:17`). Two documents stored back to back therefore share an instant and the sort is a coin flip — which is exactly what `tests/documents-db.test.ts`'s "keeps every document for a subject, newest first" was catching, at random, for weeks. **B-206 fixed the assertion, not the promise**: the test now stamps its two rows an hour apart, so it asserts the ordering `documentsFor` really offers instead of asserting that simultaneous writes have an order. **The promise itself is still unkept, and the reason this is a row rather than a defect is that it has no production caller** — `documentsFor` is referenced only by that test today, so nothing an operator sees is currently mis-ordered. It becomes real the moment a lease's document list is rendered, which is where a lien file shows its notices and where the order is evidence rather than presentation. **The fix is to let the database generate the timestamp** (`@default(dbgenerated("CURRENT_TIMESTAMP"))`), which is microsecond-resolution and makes ties vanish rather than needing a tie-break; a `cuid()` secondary sort is NOT the answer, because cuid v2 is deliberately unsortable and relying on v1's timestamp prefix is the kind of cleverness this repo decodes at 3am. **Same latent trap on every other `@default(now())` model this repo sorts by** — check `Notice`, `Message` and `AuditLog` before assuming it is one table | PRD 01 FR-4.2; PRD 02 US-16 | S | — | Phase 2 |
| 83bxk | B-222 | **The revenue report attaches its facility name with `scope="rowgroup"` too, and unlike the aging table it has no other row header at all** (found while building B-216, 2026-09-01; **verified on the code**; SC 1.3.1 A; admin). `apps/web/app/admin/reports/revenue/page.tsx:229` is the same construct B-216 removed from the aging split table — `<th scope="rowgroup" rowSpan={2}>` carrying the facility over a billed row and a collected row — and no screen reader implements it. **B-216’s fix does not transfer**, which is why this is a row rather than part of it: the aging table already had a per-row `<th scope="row">` to fold the facility into, and `RowPair` has none. Each row’s identity ("Billed", "Coll.") lives in a `<span>` *inside* the first money cell, so every one of the fourteen figures in a facility’s pair sits in a row with no header. **The cheap-looking fixes are each wrong**: `scope="row"` on the spanning cell rests on rowspan semantics nobody here has watched an AT resolve; promoting a money `<td>` to a `<th>` makes a figure into a header; an sr-only `<th>` added to the second row breaks the column count the `rowSpan` balances. **What is left is a design call**: a real row-label column carrying "Cedar Park, Billed" and "Cedar Park, Coll.", which removes the label spans from inside the cells and changes what the report looks like — the same shape of call B-217 is. `headers`/`id` on all fourteen cells is the no-visual-change alternative and is NOT recommended: it is a second AT-behaviour claim this repo would then be unable to watch, in place of one it just removed. **Whatever ships, do not re-assert an announcement nobody has heard** — that sentence is what B-216 deleted | PRD 02 §5.5; B-216; B-195 | M | B-216 | Phase 2 |
| 83bxi | B-220 ✅ | **Two month-turn defects in the reports section, found together and distinct: the pack defaults to a month in UTC while listing months facility-local, and every report's default window empties on the 1st** (found while building B-214, 2026-08-31; **verified** — both e2e failures reproduce on `main` with B-214's diff stashed, and the two default-deriving functions were probed directly at four instants). **(1) The management pack, a genuine five-hour timezone split.** `apps/web/app/admin/reports/pack/page.tsx:53` defaults to `previousMonth(new Date())`, which reads `getUTCMonth()`; `:63` then lists only periods `.ended`, which is facility-local. Probed: at `2026-09-01T00:32Z` the default is **August**, but in `America/Chicago` it was still 19:32 on 31 August and August had **not** ended — so the page rendered a pack for a month that was still running, under a name claiming all of it (which the comment at `:61-63` forbids in as many words), and the month being shown appeared in neither the nav nor `aria-current`. That is what `e2e/admin-reports.spec.ts:628` caught. The window opens at UTC midnight and closes at facility-local midnight — at `08:04Z` the same default (August) lists correctly, so the defect is invisible outside those five hours. **(2) `reportRange` defaults to the current calendar month** (`apps/web/lib/admin/report-range.ts:38-40`), so on the 1st every report opens on a month-to-date window with nothing in it yet: `/admin/reports/promotions` showed **$0.00** given against a campaign that had been discounting invoices all month, failing `admin-reports.spec.ts:196`. This is **not** bounded by the five hours — UTC only starts it early. It recurs on the 1st of every month and lasts until the new month has data. Whether the default should be the current month, the last complete month, or a rolling 30 days is a product call, and it is the call this row needs: an operator opening a report to an empty screen on the 1st has no way to tell it from a broken report. **Fix: resolve "now" in the selected facility's timezone before deriving any default range or default pack month** — the pack screen already promises a pack covers "one site's month, on that site's own timezone" (`:48-49`), so for (1) this is making the code true rather than choosing. Then settle (2) with a D-number. **The two e2e assertions are the regression test and must NOT be pinned to a fake clock**: pinning hides both defects, which is how they survived until a 19:32 run. | PRD 02 US-39; `apps/web/lib/admin/report-range.ts`; `apps/web/app/admin/reports/pack/page.tsx` | M | — | Phase 2 |
| 83bxl | B-223 | **A portfolio report has no timezone, so its default month is UTC's and can name a month still running at a US facility** (found while building B-220, 2026-09-01; **verified — `reportRange` was probed at `2026-09-01T00:32Z` against UTC and `America/Chicago` and returns August and July respectively**; admin). D-109 made every report default to the last COMPLETE calendar month, and `reportRange` takes a `timeZone` to reckon it in — but **no report page can supply one**. All seven (`revenue`, `deposits`, `funnel`, `deliverability`, `promotions`, plus `/admin/access` and `/admin/impersonation`) call `getAdminActor()` and report across every facility the actor may read, which can be several zones; none calls `resolveSelectedFacility`. So the default is UTC, and for the hours between UTC midnight and facility-local midnight on the 1st a report opens on a month that has not ended in Texas — up to five hours, more in Alaska and Hawaii. **This is strictly smaller than the defect D-109 replaced** (a window zero days wide, every 1st, for the whole day) and it is the same SHAPE as B-220 defect 1, which is why it is recorded rather than shrugged at. **The fix is not "pass a timezone"** — for a genuine multi-facility roll-up there is no single right one, and picking the operator's own would make two staff reading the same URL see different months. **The candidates are: (a) reckon completeness against the WESTERNMOST facility in scope**, so a month is complete only when it is complete everywhere the figures come from — correct by construction, needs the facility list `reportableFacilities` already computes; **(b) give the report pages the facility switcher** they do not currently use, making a single-site default meaningful and the all-facilities case fall back to (a); **(c) accept it and say so on screen** beside the range label. `SwitcherFacility` now carries `timezone` (B-220), so (b) needs no new query. **Do not pin the affected e2e assertions to a fake clock** — same instruction B-220 carried, and the same reason: pinning is what let this survive until a 19:32 run | PRD 02 US-39; D-109; B-220 | M | B-220 | Phase 2 |
| 83bxj | B-221 | **`a refused completion says why` guards only an EMPTY task queue, and failed for the first time on a full one — cause not yet identified** (found while building B-214, 2026-08-31; **NOT verified — this row needs confirmation at build time**). `e2e/admin-tasks.spec.ts:152` opens `/admin/tasks`, takes the first card with a `Complete` button, fills its note with whitespace and expects the server's refusal in `main [role=alert]`. Its only precondition guard is `test.skip(count === 0)`. In one 1310-test sweep it **self-skipped on mobile-chrome** (the queue was empty at that moment) and **failed on desktop-chrome at `:172`** (`element(s) not found`, 5s timeout) — the same test, the same database, the same run, so the queue's contents are being changed underneath it by whatever else is in flight. PROGRESS records the opposite state for B-170's session: *"the freshly seeded demo carries no open tasks, so `a refused completion says why` ran on neither project"*. So this test has never had a stable precondition; it has only ever been empty-and-skipped or, now, full-and-failing. **What is ruled out:** it is not a task type that permits a blank note — every type in `packages/core/tasks/catalog.ts` declares `requiredProofFields: ["note"]` and `DEFAULT_REQUIRED_FIELDS` is `["note"]`, so a trimmed-blank note is always missing. It is not a `resolvedByAction` card either, since `fill()` on the note placeholder succeeded. **What is not known:** why no alert rendered. The likeliest remaining reading is B-120's own failure mode — a sibling spec completing or removing that task between page load and click, so the action refused for a different reason or redirected — but that is a hypothesis, not a finding, and this repo does not record hypotheses as causes. **Fix: reproduce it in isolation first** (a clean `db:reset-test` + `db:migrate:e2e`, then this spec alone), then give the test one of B-120's three disciplines rather than a skip that only covers zero: either seed a task this spec owns and scope the mutation to it, or assert against a task the rest of the suite never touches. **Do not "fix" it by widening the skip** — a test that skips whenever it would fail asserts nothing | PRD 02 FR-19; B-120; B-141; B-170 | S | — | Phase 2 |
| 83bxm | B-224 | **A sale can be scheduled before the notice deadline, and sold with no advertisement on file** (operator review 2026-09-01, finding 1; **verified against the code** — `scheduleSale` performs no date arithmetic of any kind, and `recordSaleOutcome`'s refusal list contains no advertisement check). `scheduleSale` (`apps/web/lib/auctions/service.ts:521-556`) writes `saleDate` straight through: a date before the served notice's `deadlineDate` (`packages/db/prisma/schema.prisma:3782` — stored since the notice pipeline shipped and read by nothing in `auctionReadiness`), a date before the timeline's own steps, and a date in the past are all accepted. `recordSaleOutcome` (`:735-766`) refuses a sale with no lock-cut record and no buyer record, and does **not** refuse one with zero `AuctionAdvertisement` rows. So a manager serves the notice on the 5th giving the tenant until the 19th, schedules the sale for the 9th, cuts the lock, takes $900 and records the outcome, and the file contains no advertisement at all — the two commonest wrongful-sale claims, both of them producible by a pipeline whose whole premise is "no override, no yellow banner". **Fix: two blockers and two refusals, in the shape the existing ones already have.** `auctionReadiness` (`packages/core/auctions/readiness.ts:90-212`) gains `sale_before_notice_deadline` — `scheduledSaleDate` on or after the served notice's `deadlineDate`, reckoned **facility-local** (B-220/B-223's rule; a UTC comparison here would be wrong by a day in exactly the direction that hurts); `scheduleSale` validates the date it is handed instead of storing it; `recordSaleOutcome` refuses a sale with no `AuctionAdvertisement` dated on or before `soldAt`, and refuses a `soldAt` earlier than `scheduledSaleDate`. Every refusal names the fix, as `approveAuction`'s already do. **The interval between notice and sale is configuration, not a constant** — `minDaysNoticeToSale` on the delinquency timeline per D-10, presented as an example configuration and never as compliant (US-29); the deadline comparison itself is the hard rule and needs no number to be correct. **Do not fold this into B-129** — that row is partner-blocked and this must not wait on it — and do not let the advertisement refusal accept the lot sheet as evidence: D-104 already settled that downloading a sheet is not evidence that an advertisement ran, and D-106 settled that a missing advertisement *element* is a blank column, which this rule does not disturb | PRD 02 US-28, US-29; D-104; D-106; D-10 | M | B-062 | Phase 2 |
| 83bxn | B-225 | **Money paid ahead has nowhere to live, so the tenant who prepaid is charged a late fee and then charged again** (operator review finding 2, 2026-09-01; **verified against the code** for the allocation, late-fee and autopay paths and for the portal's own refusal comment). `applyPayment` returns `unappliedCents` (`apps/web/lib/billing/allocation.ts:270-274`) and **nothing downstream reads it**. A tenant hands the counter $600 in December for six months: $150 settles the open invoice and $450 sits nowhere. In January the new invoice is issued at full value, `assessLateFees` selects on `totalCents - amountPaidCents` per invoice with no lease-level credit awareness (`apps/web/lib/billing/late-fees.ts:70-130`) and charges a late fee, and `runAutopay` computes `outstanding = invoice.totalCents - invoice.amountPaidCents` (`apps/web/lib/billing/autopay.ts:184-236`) and takes another $150 from their card — a chargeback and a lost tenant, produced by their own money. The month-end journal has already posted the $450 to Customer Deposits (`packages/core/accounting/journal.ts:244`), so the liability is on the books with no mechanism anywhere that can discharge it, and the portal meanwhile **refuses the same overpayment outright** (`apps/web/lib/portal/payment.ts:23-27`: *"a credit balance has nowhere to live… Prepayment comes back with B-044"*) — B-044 shipped without it, which makes this the oldest unowned money gap in the tree and the reason the product will not take cash a tenant is trying to give it. **Fix: one lease-level credit, read by the three jobs that already ask "what is still owed".** Invoice generation sweeps a lease's unallocated payment money onto the new invoice before it is issued; late-fee assessment does not fee an invoice a credit covers; autopay nets the credit before it creates a PaymentIntent; the tenant profile carries a visible "Credit on account: $450" with a staff control to apply it to a named invoice, per this repo's rule that a value which changes behaviour ships with its control in the same item. Once credit exists, the portal's refusal is lifted. **What happens to a credit at move-out is not this row's call and is D-113** — build the in-life behaviour and leave the disposition branch to the decision; an unclaimed-property question is not settled by a build session (the same reasoning PRD 10 §11 already carries for referral credits) | PRD 02 US-22, US-24; PRD 01 US-703; D-113 | L | B-044, B-045, B-047 | Phase 2 |
| 83bxo | B-226 | **The facility page advertises a promotion, says it is already in the total, and leaves it out of the total** (UX review 2026-09-01, finding 1; **verified against the code**; customer-facing). Nothing to enter — it is already in the total below."* The total below is `CostBreakdown` (`:195-254`), which calls `calculateMoveInCost({ webRateCents, streetRateCents, adminFeeCents, taxRates })` at `:205-210` — **no `promoDiscountCents`, no `promoTerms`**. The page computes `firstPeriodCents` at `:805`, puts it in the `promos` map, passes it into `UnitTypeCard` (`:311`) and never renders it; checkout **does** apply it (`apps/web/components/checkout/price-summary.tsx:68-75`). So the browse estimate and the money path disagree by exactly the discount — the release-blocking defect US-301's shared-calculation criterion exists to prevent, reappearing on the other side of the same function (`packages/core/pricing/move-in-cost.ts:31-36`), and a comparison shopper who opens "What you'd pay today" under a *First month free* badge and sees full rent concludes the discount is fake. **The fix is two props**: `CostBreakdown` takes the card's promo and passes `promoDiscountCents: unitType.webRateCents - promo.firstPeriodCents` and `promoTerms: promo.terms`; `calculateMoveInCost` already emits the line, the note and the reduced taxable base. The reassurance sentence stays only on the automatic branch, where it becomes true; the code-gated branch already differs. **Add the assertion US-301 claims exists and does not**: the facility page's `totalDueTodayCents` for a promo'd unit type equals `amountDueToday` for a checkout session on the same unit type and code. `firstPeriodCents` must have exactly one consumer afterwards — an unread money field on a card is how this happened. Then branch the disclosure on `autopayOn`, using `/portal/methods:134-141` as the model since it already branches correctly. A test asserts the `/portal/methods` figure equals the disclosure the tenant signed; a difference is a defect, not a rounding note. **The two submit buttons on the money step are in scope**: make it unambiguous that unticking without pressing Save does nothing, or make the checkbox self-submitting and drop the button. WCAG 2.1 AA: the outcome announces in words ("Automatic payments turned off") from a `role="status"` region that is in the DOM before the submit (4.1.3), never as a silently re-rendered checkbox; the recurring figure is labelled text, not a placeholder (3.3.2) | PRD 01 US-601, US-704, US-301; D-11a; D-20 | M | B-103 | Phase 2 |
| 83bxq | B-228 | **The same payment-plan installment is due on two different days, one tap apart** (UX review finding 4, 2026-09-01; **verified against the code** — two call sites pass a timezone and two do not, over a value stored at UTC midnight; **the direction of the shift needs confirmation at build time** against the deployed runtime's `TZ`). Installment dates are written by `parseDate` (`apps/web/lib/admin/form-state.ts:116-123`), which does `new Date("2026-10-15")` — UTC midnight — so in `America/Chicago` the dashboard says **14 October** while the schedule and the staffer who typed it both say **15 October**. | 83bxr | B-229 | **A nightly job that fails writes a row and tells nobody, and the one screen that shows it speaks in job identifiers and cuids** (operator review finding 3 merged with UX review finding 11, 2026-09-01; **verified against the code** — `runner.ts`'s catch block has no side effect beyond the row, no consumer of `jobRun.status === 'failed'` exists outside `/admin/billing`, and `SCHEDULED_JOBS` entries carry no label field, so one has to be added). `packages/core/jobs/runner.ts:109-115` writes `status: 'failed'` and returns: no task, no event, no message. FR-1 already promises a notification bell carrying failed runs and nothing builds it. When somebody does open the screen, `apps/web/app/admin/billing/page.tsx:71` renders `run.jobName` verbatim — `billing.assess-late-fees`, `delinquency.payment-plan-breach`, `access.drain-commands`, `field-ops.raise-walkthrough` (`apps/web/lib/jobs/registry.ts:251,345,365,384,440,497`) — and the per-item failure list at `:83-90` renders `item.itemId`, a raw cuid, as the only identifier: *"Failed · clx8s0a9b0001xyz — card declined"*. That is B-109's rule (admin may use the industry's vocabulary, never the codebase's identifiers) unswept on the screen whose own comment at `:9-13` says it exists so a 2am failure is not indistinguishable from a quiet night. **Fix, one session, alarm first: a failed or partial `JobRun` raises a high-priority `Task` at that facility**, a new `job_failed` type in the catalog (`packages/core/tasks/catalog.ts` holds twenty types and none of them is this), naming the job and the error, idempotent on (type, entityId, businessDate) like every other task; the "Across your facilities" roll-up carries an unresolved job-failure count per site; a facility with no successful run of a money job in 48 hours escalates. **Then make the screen readable**: every `SCHEDULED_JOBS` entry gains a `label` an operator would say out loud ("Assess late fees", "Check payment plans for missed installments", "Send gate commands") and the table renders it; failure items name their subject the way task cards do — tenant name, unit number, linked to the profile, since `recordItem` already receives the entity id; "OK 412 / Failed 3" links straight to the three; and an id that genuinely cannot be resolved prints "unknown record" beside it as an honest fallback rather than as the default. **Sweep one more internal string while in here**: `apps/web/app/admin/[section]/page.tsx:31` tells staff *"This screen is built in a later backlog item"* — delete the route if it is dead, reword it if it is not. | 83bxs | B-230 | **The counter cannot take a card, a walk-in move-in cannot take cash or a check, and every counter rental is recorded as a web rental** (operator review findings 4 and 8 merged, 2026-09-01; **verified against the code** for both refusals, for the checkout page's single payment component and for the `'web'` fallback). `recordCounterPayment` refuses `card` outright (`apps/web/lib/admin/pos.ts:103-104`) and the POS form offers cash/check/money order only (`apps/web/app/admin/pos/page.tsx:162-165`), so the delinquent tenant standing at the counter with a debit card, wanting their gate to reopen, is told *"Card payments go through the online payment screen — there is no card terminal here yet"* (`apps/web/app/admin/pos/actions.ts:24`) — a deflection to email aimed at precisely the tenant least likely to read it. `startWalkInMoveInAction` redirects staff into the public `/checkout`, whose only payment step is the Stripe Payment Element, so **there is no cash or check option on a move-in at all**, which US-32's own AC has required since it was written and B-039 closed without. 'web'` (`apps/web/lib/checkout/provision.ts:234`), so the walk-in half of the business — a third to a half of move-ins at most sites — reports as organic web and marketing is priced against it; `apps/web/lib/admin/reports.ts:514-547` splits correctly with no change once the value is right. **Fix: the tender step, which is the only piece that is missing.** A keyed card-not-present charge through the existing `createChargeIntent` needs no terminal and closes most of it — **entered through the Stripe Element on the counter screen, never a PAN typed into a form of ours**, because US-601's SAQ-A boundary is not negotiable for a staff-operated screen; a "charge the card on file" control on the tenant profile, which today carries no payment control at all, closes the rest and is the commonest counter request there is. Change calculation (`packages/core/pos/tender.ts`), gapless receipt numbering (D-22), drawer sessions and the non-overridable staff attribution on cash (US-32) are all built and unchanged, and **the configurable cash ceiling still escalates to a manager (RBAC-2)** — the tender step does not become a way around it. | 83bxt | B-231 | **The counter takes payments on a screen that never shows what the tenant owes, and cannot take one from a former tenant at all** (UX review finding 5 merged with operator review finding 5, 2026-09-01; **verified against the code**). `apps/web/app/admin/pos/page.tsx:138-196`: staff search, pick a tenant, and get Unit (a select of bare unit numbers, `:155-161`), Method, and **Amount ($)** as free text with no reference figure anywhere on the screen — the balance is neither queried nor rendered. A walk-in payment is therefore search → open the profile in another tab → read the figure → come back → retype it, and a tenant with two units gets the money applied to whichever unit the staffer guesses. The same picker scopes leases to `OCCUPYING_LEASE_STATUSES` (`:52`), which excludes `ended`, so the former-tenant AR that `/admin/tenants/former` lists — under its own header saying *"A read, not a queue: collections"* — is **100% uncollectable inside the product**: a former tenant who walks in with $400 in cash cannot be given a receipt for it, even though `recordCounterPayment`'s lease lookup has no status filter (`apps/web/lib/admin/pos.ts:109-120`) and would take it today. **This is a screen change, not a service change.** The selected-tenant card shows each lease with its unit number and current balance, oldest due first; the `<option>` labels carry the balance (`B-114 — $312.40 due`); a "Pay in full — $312.40" button fills the Amount field and leaves it editable, exactly as B-212's even split does for the plan builder; the aging is stated in words beside it ("$312.40, 41 days past due") so the person taking the cash knows whether to mention the overlock before the tenant leaves; ended leases carrying a balance appear, visually separated and labelled as a former tenant, and `/admin/tenants/former` gains a "take payment" link per row. **The permission half is D-110 and this row is blocked on that, not on code**: `counter` does not hold `reports:financial` (`packages/db/rbac-catalog.ts:151-165`), while the tenant profile shows `lease.balanceCents` under `tenants:view`, which counter does hold (`apps/web/app/admin/tenants/[tenantId]/page.tsx:505`, `:546`) — so the figure is already available to this person one screen away, and the gate as it stands looks like an accident rather than a policy. **The remote half is deliberately not in this row**: a pay link that survives move-out would reverse D-30, which revokes them at move-out, so it is raised as **D-112** rather than written here | PRD 02 US-32, US-13; D-110; D-30 | M | B-039, B-055 | Phase 2 |
| 83bxu | B-232 | **The portal asks a past-due tenant for money without saying what it is for, or what paying it buys** (UX review findings 6 and 7 merged, 2026-09-01; **verified against the code**; customer-facing). `apps/web/app/portal/pay/page.tsx:103-112` is a two-row `<dl>` — "Balance $487.50", "Paying today $487.50" — with no line items, while a past-due balance is rent plus a late fee plus possibly a lien-prep fee and a second month's rent, and *what is this* is the question that gets asked before anybody pays; `/portal/statements` covers **closed months only** and lists a month label and the word "View" with no amount (`apps/web/app/portal/statements/page.tsx:66-70`), so it does not answer it either, and neither does the dashboard. On the same two screens the gate story is wrong in both directions. `apps/web/app/portal/page.tsx:54-70` hardcodes *"Pay your full balance of $487.50 and your gate code starts working again, usually within a couple of minutes"*, while the rule is a per-facility threshold — `packages/core/access/suspension.ts:72` restores when `balanceCents <= restoreAtOrBelowCents`, from `Facility.accessRestoreAtOrBelowCents` (`packages/db/prisma/schema.prisma:692`, default 0), which `portalDashboardForTenant` does not select — so a site that sets $50 demands $487.50 for what $437.50 would buy. And `/portal/pay`'s "Pay a different amount" (`:120-141`) says **nothing** about a partial payment leaving the gate shut, which is the wasted trip and the angriest call the office takes. **This does not re-open D-16**, whose own text stores the threshold per facility precisely so it can be relaxed; it makes the copy read the setting instead of restating the default. **Fix: itemise the current balance from the same ledger read that produced the total** — rent for the period, late fee with the date it was assessed and in the tenant's words ("Late fee, assessed 6 October", never the ledger's reason code), protection premium, tax — in the `<dl>` shape the checkout payment step already uses; statement rows carry their closing balance so a bookkeeper can find a month without opening five; the dashboard reads `accessRestoreAtOrBelowCents` and names the real number; `/portal/pay` states live beside the amount field what the entered amount does to gate access (*"$100.00 will not reopen your gate. $437.50 will"*), with the copy unchanged where the threshold is 0; and the suspension comms template uses the same sentence so the screen and the message cannot diverge. **One clause of copy from a finding that stays declined**: the past-due banner may say "or call {phone} to ask about splitting it into payments", which describes what staff can already do and commits to no tenant-initiated plan policy — the request feature itself remains declined exactly as it was in the B-187–B-196 block, and PRD 01 §9 still carries it as an open gap. WCAG 2.1 AA: the itemisation is a real `<dl>` or table with headers rather than a visual list (1.3.1); the live consequence sentence sits in a `role="status"` region present before input (4.1.3); a disputable fee's phone number is a link on that line, not only at the foot of the page (2.4.4) | PRD 01 US-702, US-703, US-705, §6.7; D-16 | M | B-098, B-049 | Phase 2 |
| 83bxv | B-233 | **Task assignment is built, tested and reachable from nothing, so "my day" is everyone's day** (UX review finding 8, 2026-09-01; **verified against the code**). `apps/web/lib/admin/tasks.ts:418-428` exports `assignTask`, permission-checked and covered by `tests/tasks-db.test.ts:366-377`, and **its only callers are those tests**. `/admin/tasks` (`apps/web/app/admin/tasks/page.tsx:130-132`) and `/admin/delinquency` (`apps/web/app/admin/delinquency/page.tsx:146-148`) both render `{task.assigneeName ? 'Assigned to X' : 'Unassigned'}` as static text: no claim button, no assign control, no "just mine" filter. US-41 is called "my day" and what shipped is the facility's day — two staff on a Saturday work one undifferentiated list and either cut the same lock twice or both skip it, on the queue that drives the lien clock and the 95%-of-steps-on-their-day success metric. This is CLAUDE.md's first hard-learned rule again: a column that configures behaviour, shipped without its control. **Fix: "Take this" on each unassigned card calling `assignTask(actor, taskId, actor.staffUserId)`, "Give back" on one assigned to the reader, and a three-state filter — Mine / Unassigned / Everyone — defaulting to mine first and unassigned second, which is the order a person works.** The heading carries the count the reader is responsible for ("Tasks — Cedar Park · 4 yours, 11 unassigned"). **Manager reassignment to another named staffer is deliberately out of this row**: claiming is the 90% case and needs no picker, and a picker is a second permission question rather than a second control. WCAG 2.1 AA: each button's accessible name names its own card's subject, per US-41's existing 2.4.6/4.1.3 criterion ("Take Complete returned-mail review for Ada Renter, unit 104") rather than the same bare verb once per card; the claim outcome announces from the page-level region B-170 built, which survives the card it reports on (4.1.3); the filter is a named group of controls and the list's new length is stated in words rather than silently re-rendered | PRD 02 US-41 | M | B-095, B-170 | Phase 2 |
| 83bxw | B-234 | **An auction surplus becomes overdue a year later and nothing anywhere says so** (operator review finding 6, 2026-09-01; **verified against the code** — no `surplus` reference in `apps/web/lib/jobs/registry.ts` or in the task catalog). `surplusHoldUntil` is set to sale + 365 days (`packages/core/auctions/surplus.ts:31`) and `surplusObligation` already computes both the not-yet-notified state and the overdue one; the only consumer is `outstandingSurpluses`, rendered on `/admin/auctions` for the **currently selected single facility** (`apps/web/app/admin/auctions/page.tsx:41-44`) — a screen nobody opens at a site with no live cases. The surplus notification to the former tenant is equally unprompted. US-28's own AC says a surplus is a liability with a statutory life, and the module's own comment says quietly retaining one is how a routine auction becomes a class-action-shaped problem: what is missing is the alarm, not the arithmetic. **Fix, small: a nightly job raises a `Task` when a sold case has a surplus and `surplusTenantNotifiedAt` is null** — within days of the sale, not a year later — a second at `surplusHoldUntil` minus a configurable lead, and an escalating one once `overdue` is true. Overdue surpluses surface on the owner dashboard roll-up and in the management pack, across facilities, because a single-facility screen is what failed here. The task carries the amount and the former tenant, since the action itself is off-system: a cheque, or a comptroller filing. **The Texas holding period stays configuration and stays labelled an example configuration** (US-29, D-10) — this row alarms on whatever it is set to and asserts nothing about what it should be | PRD 02 US-28, US-29; D-10 | S | B-062, B-095 | Phase 2 |
| 83bxx | B-235 | **The approvals the product itself requires have no cross-facility worklist** (operator review finding 7, 2026-09-01; **verified against the code** — every `mode !== 'single'` screen enumerated and cross-checked against roll-up imports). `/admin/auctions` refuses "All facilities" with a bare sentence and no roll-up (`apps/web/app/admin/auctions/page.tsx:32-38`), and `/admin/rate-increases` does the same (`:108-115`) — so the regional over ten sites who must approve every lien sale and every ECRI batch switches facility ten times every morning with no signal about which sites have anything waiting. **Thirty-two admin screens hard-refuse all-facilities and five have a roll-up behind the refusal**, which is US-1/US-2's own criterion — "in the All facilities context, every roll-up-capable screen renders a roll-up, not a refusal" — unmet on the screens where the refusal costs the most. Staff time is the first cost; the compliance edge is real, because an auction approval sitting unseen past its sale date means the advertisement ran for a sale that did not happen, which is its own claim. **Fix: `FacilityRollup` in all-facilities mode on `/admin/auctions` and `/admin/rate-increases` first, then `/admin/overlocks`, `/admin/walkthrough`, `/admin/tenants/former` and `/admin/reports/protection`.** The pattern exists and is already used on `/admin`, `/admin/tasks`, `/admin/delinquency`, `/admin/leads` and `/admin/units` (`apps/web/lib/admin/rollups.ts`), so this is application rather than design. **Rows count what is pending this actor's approval, not what merely exists**, so an empty site reads as empty rather than as unvisited, and the drill-in per facility stays — the roll-up is a router, not a merged list. WCAG 2.1 AA: each row's link names its facility and what is waiting there (2.4.4/2.4.6), and a count of zero is stated in words rather than left as an em dash, per US-2's existing criterion | PRD 02 US-1, US-2 | M | B-113 | Phase 2 |
| 83bxy | B-236 | **The hourly cron runs every facility serially inside one 300-second request, and what it drops is never retried** (operator review finding 9, 2026-09-01; **the serial shape, the 300s cap and the `facilitiesDueAt` behaviour are verified against the code; the throughput at which it actually times out needs confirmation at build time** — time one hour-0 tick against a seeded 40-facility database before sizing the fix, exactly as the reviewer asks). `/api/cron` loops `SCHEDULED_JOBS` × facilities × missed business dates, serially, against a remote Postgres, inside one request with `maxDuration = 300` (`apps/web/app/api/cron/route.ts:20`, `:87-127`). At hour 0 local every Central-time facility arrives at once; when the cap is hit, the jobs late in the list never run **and are not retried that night**, because `facilitiesDueAt` returns only facilities whose local clock is *at* the target hour, so the next tick has nothing due. Invoicing for the tail of a portfolio slips a day, every night, and the lien clock runs a day slow behind it. FR-13 states a 30-minute budget for 20 facilities and FR-14 promises catch-up runs; neither is bounded by anything in the code. **Fix: bound the work per invocation and make dueness durable.** Process a queue of due (job, facility, business date) tuples against a time budget and leave the remainder for the next tick rather than dropping it; decouple "is this due" from "is it this exact hour", so a due-and-not-yet-run tuple stays due until it runs; parallelise per facility with bounded concurrency, which is safe because the jobs are already idempotent per (job, facility, business date); and record that a run hit its budget, so an operator sees a backlog rather than nothing. **That last part is what makes B-229's alarm mean anything** — an unfinished run and a failed one must not look alike. At two to five sites this is invisible, which is the argument for fixing it before a portfolio arrives rather than after | PRD 02 FR-4, FR-13, FR-14 | M | B-229 | Phase 2 |
| 83bxz | B-237 | **There is no way to create a facility, and one created by hand takes rent and does nothing else** (operator review finding 10, 2026-09-01; **verified against the code** — `prisma.facility.create` appears only in `packages/db/prisma/seed.mts`, and there is no create route under `/admin`). `/admin/settings` is read-and-update against a facility that already exists, so onboarding a site the operator has just bought is a database session — and the silent half is the dangerous one: a hand-inserted facility has no `DelinquencyTimeline`, no `LateFeeRule`, no `FeeSchedule`, no tax component and no notice templates, so it invoices rent, charges no late fees, runs no ladder, and `auctionReadiness` blocks every sale with `no_timeline`, without a single error anywhere. B-008's row says "Facility settings CRUD"; what shipped is RU. **Fix: a create-facility flow gated on `org:defaults`, capturing what `Facility` requires (address and geo, timezone, state), which pushes the org defaults B-079 already built — fee schedule, late-fee ladder, delinquency timeline — into the new site as ordinary effective-dated rows.** That is D-41's "pushed, never resolved" shape applied at birth, so nothing new has to resolve anything at runtime and the new site's own history says where its values came from. **Plus a "this facility is not ready to operate" banner listing what is still missing, until nothing is** — the load-bearing half, because a site that bills rent and collects nothing else looks healthy on the dashboard for a month. **State is required at creation rather than later** (D-10, US-29): a facility with no state cannot carry a compliant timeline and must not be able to reach the lien pipeline at all. WCAG 2.1 AA: the readiness banner is text with a link to each missing setting rather than a colour badge (1.4.1), and the create form's server refusals reach the fields that caused them with a focused summary (3.3.1/3.3.3). **Accessibility criteria added 2026-09-01 from that block's late accessibility review, this being the largest new form in the block**: a facility that will invoice real tenants is a financial and legal commitment, so **SC 3.3.4 Error Prevention (AA)** is satisfied by the **confirm-and-echo step already shipped at `/admin/settings`** and recorded as the `tax rate confirm-and-echo` entry in `SCANNED_STATES` — **reuse it, do not invent a second pattern**; every control goes through `Field`/`FieldSet` so `aria-invalid` and `aria-describedby` cannot be omitted (§6.8's error-identification rule); the timezone `<select>` needs `max-w-full` + `min-w-0`, which `Field` already provides and a hand-rolled control does not — **B-201's exact defect**; and **the new route joins `ADMIN_SCAN_ROUTES` and its refused state joins `SCANNED_STATES` in the same commit**, not in a follow-up | PRD 02 US-3, US-4, US-29; D-41; D-10 | M | B-008, B-079 | Phase 2 |
| 83bya | B-238 | **A renter with no email address cannot be leased, and two people cannot share one** (operator review finding 11, 2026-09-01; **the schema constraint is verified against the code** — `Tenant.email` is a non-nullable `String @unique`, `packages/db/prisma/schema.prisma:1121`; **the blast radius across the comms and portal paths needs confirmation at build time**, so enumerate every reader of `Tenant.email` before sizing this). The contractor who rents a 10×20 and has no email, the elderly tenant whose daughter handles it, and the husband and wife who each want their own account on one household address all fail at the counter — and the workaround is the harmful part: staff type `nobody@example.com`, and then the dunning ladder, the receipt, the lien-notice supplement and the pay link all go into a hole, **on the account most likely to end up in a lien file**. **This row is blocked on D-111 rather than on code**: whether email stops being required and unique is an identity decision with a sign-in consequence, not a defect a build session may settle. It is written for either answer. If email becomes nullable: phone or postal address is the alternative contact of record; the unique constraint is dropped or scoped so a shared household address is allowed; sign-in requires a non-null email only where a portal login actually exists (`resolveAudience`, D-47); and the comms layer gains a defined behaviour for a tenant with no email, which is the existing `no_reachable_channel` task type rather than a silent skip. Notice generation already snapshots the postal address it rendered (D-21), so the lien path never depended on email in the first place. If email stays required, the counter needs a stated, non-fabricated answer instead — a facility-owned placeholder domain that the comms layer refuses to send to and that raises the same task — and that answer is part of the decision too. **Accessibility criterion added 2026-09-01 from that block's late accessibility review, and it binds whichever way D-111 goes**: the counter form's email field stops being required, so the consequence is stated as a programmatic instruction on the control — a `hint` on the `Field`, which wires into `aria-describedby` (**SC 3.3.2 Labels or Instructions, A**) — **naming what the tenant loses without an email: no receipt, no pay link, no dunning notice, no lien-notice supplement**. Removing `required` and leaving the field silently optional is the version that produces `nobody@example.com`, which is the harm this row is about | PRD 02 US-13; PRD 05 CN-19; D-111; D-47; D-21 | L | B-038 | Phase 2 |
| 83byb | B-239 | **The move-in confirmation hands the renter nothing to do next, and the portal's one permanent action is Move out** (UX review findings 9 and 13 merged, 2026-09-01; **verified against the code**; customer-facing). `apps/web/app/(public)/checkout/page.tsx:645-698` shows the gate code, unit number, address, today's gate hours, directions and a link back to the facility page: **the words "portal", "account" and "password" do not appear anywhere in the file**, and neither does the next charge — although the welcome email carries both (`packages/db/comms-catalog.ts:78-104`, `{{links.portal}}` and `{{billing.first_charge_line}}`), and although the details step has already told the renter *"This is your account… no password needed"* (`apps/web/components/checkout/details-step.tsx:87`) before never showing it to them. **US-601's existing criterion — the confirmation screen restates the autopay state, the next charge date and the next charge amount — is unmet**, and portal activation currently depends entirely on an email being opened. One screen later the same tenant meets `apps/web/app/portal/layout.tsx:66-111`: the persistent nav is Overview · Payment methods · Statements · Documents · (Payment plan) · Manage ▾ · **Move out**, while paying is reachable only from the lease card on Overview (`apps/web/app/portal/page.tsx:35-44`) — so the one irreversible destination in the product holds permanent top-level space and the one thing collections depends on does not. The comment at `:73-77` argues Move out is separated *because* it is irreversible, which is an argument for putting it inside Manage beside "Move to another unit", not beside Overview. **Fix: a primary "Go to my account" action under the gate code**, using the session checkout already established; the next charge in words (*"Your next payment is $167.36 on 1 November. Autopay is on — we'll email you two days before"*), suppressed or reworded where they opted out at the payment step; one line on what to bring (*"Bring your own lock, or buy one at the office"*), the top post-move-in call, with the merchandise screen already built; and "Didn't arrive? Check spam, or call {facility phone}" beside the emailed-lease sentence. **Then "Pay" becomes the first nav item whenever any lease has a balance > 0**, carrying the amount (**Pay $487.50**) and linking to `/portal/pay?lease=…` for one lease or a chooser for several, with Move out moved into Manage so the row stays at four or fewer top-level items — B-117's constraint, which is why Pay **replaces** Move out rather than adding to it. On a phone a past-due tenant gets the pay action as a sticky bottom bar across `/portal/*`, not only as a card on Overview. WCAG 2.1 AA: the sticky bar must not obscure content at 320px reflow (1.4.10) and sits in DOM order for a keyboard user (2.4.3); "Go to my account" is a real link whose name says where it goes (2.4.4); the next-charge sentence is text rather than a badge (1.4.1). **Accessibility review finding 7 folded in here 2026-09-01 rather than taking a row, because this row already rewrites `apps/web/app/portal/layout.tsx`'s nav** (**verified against the code**): **no link in the portal nav carries `aria-current` at all** — not the four top-level links at `:69-84`, not Payment plan at `:82`, not the six inside Manage — so a tenant on `/portal/refer`, reachable only from inside the collapsed Manage menu, has no programmatic indication of where they are, on the one navigation the customer uses. Make the nav a client component with `usePathname()` or thread the pathname down, and set `aria-current={active ? 'page' : undefined}` on **all eleven links, the Pay link this row adds included**; and give the Manage `<details>` at `:86` `open` when the active route is one of its six, mirroring `apps/web/components/admin/side-nav.tsx:62`, which already does both. **This is beyond AA — 2.4.8 Location is AAA — and is taken because it is a two-line fix on a customer surface whose pattern is already written in this repo, not because the gate requires it.** **The handed-over `<details>`-closes-on-navigation item was REFUSED by the accessibility reviewer as a WCAG failure and nothing is built for it**: 3.2.3 Consistent Navigation (AA) is met (a disclosure resetting to its default state is a state, not a reordering, and the menu is closed on every page, which is consistent), 4.1.2 (A) is met (native `<summary>` exposes `button` with expanded/collapsed), 2.4.3 concerns focus after navigation, which is the router's business and not the menu's, and 2.4.8 is AAA. **B-247 raises the six Manage links' tap-target heights separately** — same file, same links, a §6.2 gate miss rather than a conformance one; whichever lands first, the other rebases onto it | PRD 01 US-501 step 7, US-601, US-703, §6.5; B-117 | M | B-117 | Phase 2 |
| 83byc | B-240 | **The tenant profile is 2,086 lines in one column with no way to reach anything** (UX review finding 10, 2026-09-01; **verified against the code**, with the header region `:200-320` checked specifically for tabs and a summary and having neither). `apps/web/app/admin/tenants/[tenantId]/page.tsx` runs fourteen sections in fixed order — access (`:311`), holds (`:363`), leases (`:435`), payment plans (`:581`), fees (`:720`), contact (`:793`), address (`:879`), referrals (`:1006`), notes (`:1076`), documents (`:1156`), gate history (`:1243`), comms (`:1272`), actions (`:1311`) — with no in-page navigation and no sticky summary, so "waive this fee for the person in front of me" is a long scroll past referral history and gate events, worst on the phone viewport B-217 established is a real staff viewport for this exact screen. **Fix: a sticky one-line summary under the name — total balance, days past due, unit count, any hold, the four facts every visit starts with — and an anchor list under the heading in the order a counter uses them: Balance & leases · Take action · Payment plans · Fees · Contact · History.** **The section ORDER is D-95's and this row does not change it.** D-95 weighed exactly this trade and refused "money and actions near the top" on the ground that the read stack is the thing being defended; a second reviewer now says that order costs counter time on every interaction, so the reversal is raised as **D-114** and is not written here. An anchor list is the answer that does not need the reversal: one click to Actions from the top of the page, with the read stack intact. Gate history and communication history are already meant to show five and hide the rest behind a named disclosure (D-95) — **confirm at build time whether that shipped**, and if it did, put their counts in the sticky summary rather than adding a second disclosure. WCAG 2.1 AA: the anchor list is a named `<nav>` whose links move focus to the target heading rather than only scrolling (2.4.1, 2.4.3); the sticky summary must not obscure content at 320px reflow or 200% zoom (1.4.10, 1.4.4); each `<summary>` names what it opens and its count (2.4.6), per D-95's existing position. **Accessibility criteria added 2026-09-01 from that block's late accessibility review, which names this row's two shapes as the highest-risk in the whole block and noted that the row carried none**: (1) the sticky summary **must not obscure content at 320px reflow or at 200% zoom** (**1.4.10 AA, 1.4.4 AA**) — verify with `measureThreeWays` and add `/admin/tenants/[tenantId] \| sticky summary` to `STATE_REACH`, because a bar eating 15% of a 512px-tall zoomed viewport is a reflow failure and not a design choice; (2) the in-page navigation is a **real `<nav aria-label="On this profile">` of links** to the fourteen `id`s the sections already carry via `aria-labelledby` (**1.3.1 A**), and activating one **moves focus to the target heading** (`tabIndex={-1}` + `focus()`) rather than scrolling only — a scroll-only jump leaves a screen-reader user's virtual cursor exactly where it was (**2.4.3 A**); (3) the summary's "days past due" and "total balance" are a **`<dl>` of labelled pairs, not two bare numbers**, and **are not a live region** — see B-245 | PRD 02 US-13; D-95; D-114; B-217 | M | B-217 | Phase 2 |
| 83byd | B-241 | **Sixteen reports in one flat row, under a navigation label that is wrong for a third of them** (UX review finding 12, 2026-09-01; **verified against the code**; admin). `apps/web/app/admin/reports/page.tsx:242-352` is a single `<nav aria-label="Financial reports">` holding sixteen equally weighted button links, four of which are not financial reports — Indexation (`:277`), Structured data (`:331`), Duplicate content (`:337`), Deliverability (`:343`) — and two of which are operational (Waitlist `:312`, Uncovered units `:322`). The accessible name is therefore false, which makes this a naming defect rather than a layout preference, and an owner opening Reports to ask "what's owed and what's empty" meets a wall of chips with no ranking while the two figures the page already computes sit below the fold. **Fix: three named groups with honest names — Money (revenue, delinquency aging, plans & holds, deposits, monthly close, management pack, KPI trend), Operations (funnel, promotions, waitlist, uncovered units, subscriptions), Marketing & comms (indexation, structured data, duplicate content, deliverability) — each `<nav>` or `<section>` carrying its own accurate name, Money first and open, the other two permitted to be `<details>`; and the occupancy and AR figures already computed on this page move above the link wall.** The links are navigation; the numbers are the answer. **Related to B-222 and distinct**: that row is the revenue table's markup, this is the index's structure, and neither blocks the other. WCAG 2.1 AA: each group's name matches its contents (2.4.6, 4.1.2); the groups are headings-plus-lists rather than a flat run of buttons (1.3.1). **Accessibility criteria added 2026-09-01 from that block's late accessibility review, and stated here so the row cannot be descoped as polish**: the false `<nav aria-label="Financial reports">` is **SC 4.1.2 Name, Role, Value (A)** on the landmark and **SC 2.4.6 Headings and Labels (AA)** on the label — a naming failure, not a layout preference. Each of the three groups gets **its own `<nav>` with its own accurate `aria-label`**, or one `<nav>` containing three `<h2>`-headed `<ul>`s; **the group headings are real headings, not styled `<div>`s** | PRD 02 US-39, §5.5 | S | — | Phase 2 |
| 83bye | B-242 | **A search result quotes a price with no size attached, and carries no photo** (UX review finding 14, 2026-09-01; **the card's fields are verified against the code**; **whether `searchFacilities` can carry the cheapest type's dimensions and one photo without a second query per result needs confirmation at build time** — check the query shape before sizing, and **this row inherits its reviewer's MEDIUM confidence**, the way B-181 did). `apps/web/app/(public)/storage/search/page.tsx:102-151` renders name, distance, address, amenity chips and *"Units from $60/mo"*. US-101's criterion is met literally and the renter's question is not: a 5×5 at $60 and a 10×10 at $60 are entirely different decisions, and a site whose cheapest unit is a locker reads as cheaper than one whose cheapest is a garage — on the list that is the denominator of every rate below it. There is no image on any card either, while `FacilityPhoto` rows exist and B-118 established that a renter comparing three sites judges "clean, lit, not a dump" from a photo; the facility page got that treatment and the list that ranks facilities did not. **Fix: the price names its size** (*"10×10 from $60/mo · 4 more sizes"*), which `fromWebRateCents` already knows because it comes from a specific unit type; **one lazy thumbnail per card** from the facility's first photo, and never a placeholder frame where there is no photo, matching the facility page's own rule; **availability in the vocabulary the facility page already uses** ("6 sizes available", "Only 2 units left") from the real count. **No badge without a number and no fabricated scarcity** — the reviewer confirmed the product has none today and this row must not introduce any. The "No units available right now — call" branch is right and stays. WCAG 2.1 AA: the thumbnail is decorative beside a link that already names the facility, so `alt=""` rather than a repeated name (1.1.1); size and price stay text and survive 200% zoom without truncation (1.4.4); the card link's accessible name carries facility and distance (2.4.4); the lexicon is D-15's — **size**, not "unit type". **Accessibility criteria added 2026-09-01 from that block's late accessibility review; this is customer-facing, so §6.8 and §6.8.1 bind**: a facility photo that adds no information carries **`alt=""` and is not itself a link** (**1.1.1 A**) — if it is the card's only link target it takes the facility name as its accessible name; the cheapest type's dimensions use the **two-span pattern already shipped in B-016** (`10` / `×` / `sr-only` "10 foot by 10 foot"), because §6.8.1's own row records that U+00D7 announces as a multiplication operator and **this card would be the fourth surface to reintroduce it**; and price-and-size is **one sentence in the accessible name**, not two adjacent nodes — "Units from $60/mo" and "5×5" read separately tell the renter nothing about the relationship between them | PRD 01 US-101, US-301, §6.10; D-15; B-118 | M | B-118 | Phase 2 |
| 83byf | B-243 | **A certified letter that comes back is nobody's work item** (operator review finding 12, 2026-09-01; **verified against the code** that nothing reads a tracking status back; **whether the configured provider offers a status API or a webhook needs confirmation at build time, and D-63's no-simulator rule applies — build it against a real key or do not build it**). The product's legal position is correct and is not touched by this row: service by certified mail is complete on mailing, and `recordNoticeDelivery` records the mailing date rather than receipt (`apps/web/lib/notices/service.ts:808-816`). What is missing is the operational read. The tracking numbers stored in `Notice.deliveryProof` are never asked about again, so a lien notice returned *unclaimed* or *moved, left no address* — the strongest signal there is that the address of record is stale — teaches the operator nothing, and the only returned-mail path in the product is a staff member clicking a button on the tenant profile after physically handling an envelope. **Fix: poll the provider, or consume its webhook, for the stored tracking numbers; store the outcome beside the mailing date without touching the service date; raise the existing `returned_mail_review` task at high priority for a returned or unclaimed LIEN notice, naming the notice, so the alternate contact is tried before the sale; and show the tracking outcome on the auction case screen next to the served notice**, because a manager approving a sale should see that the letter came back even though it does not invalidate service. It is the difference between a defensible file and a persuasive one, and it is how a stale address gets corrected before the next notice. **This is last in the block because it may not be buildable at all**: unconfigured is the shipped state (D-63), and if the provider exposes no status the honest outcome is a row that stays open rather than a simulator that manufactures one | PRD 02 US-30, US-28, US-13; D-63; D-21 | M | B-083 | Phase 2 |

and the twelve `Field`s at `:206-222` are named "Due" and "Amount ($)" twelve times over, with the ordinal living only in the `<legend>` at `:203`, which is announced on entering the group and not when a rotor jump lands on control seventeen. **Fix: delete the coalescing claim and replace it with what is structurally true, per B-216; debounce the region's text or drive it off `onBlur` rather than `onChange` so one field edit produces one announcement; add an `aria-label` naming the ordinal on the two `Field`s ("Installment 3 due date" / "Installment 3 amount") while keeping the visible `<label>` text as-is, so the rotor list is not twenty-four identical entries; What is not contested is that each one is a focus stop that announces nothing). `apps/web/components/admin/ar-aging-split-table.tsx:70`; `apps/web/app/admin/reports/plans-holds/page.tsx:230`, `:338`; `apps/web/app/globals.css:181` (the pattern); 52 more.
