| 83bym | B-250 | **The accessibility statement's review date is structurally frozen, and B-218 flipped one of its sentences without a record** (accessibility review finding 8, 2026-09-01; **verified against the code and against `docs/PROGRESS.md`**; **not a WCAG criterion — PRD 01 §6.8**, "the accessibility statement claims only what is true today … the page carries a dated 'last reviewed' line"). `apps/web/app/(public)/accessibility/page.tsx:14`, `:218-219`, `:289`, `:317`. The comment log in that file is exemplary — B-203, B-205, B-207, B-208, B-210, B-214, B-215, B-216, B-217 and B-220 all re-read it — and it does not cover two things. **(1) `LAST_REVIEWED = '19 August 2026'`, nineteen merged items ago.** The per-item rule — bump only when a claim was re-checked against the build — is correct in itself and, applied item by item, **guarantees the date never moves**, because no single item ever re-checks the whole page; the sentence it dates is "These are the problems we know about, as of 19 August 2026", which a September visitor reads as a September statement of known shortfalls. **(2) B-218 changed the truth value of a claim and there is no B-218 entry in the file**: `:218-219` says automated tests run *"on every pull request that is open for review"*, which was false for the entire life of the split lanes — `ready_for_review` fired no workflow and all sixteen PRs reported `e2e=skipping` — until B-218 made it true. The page's own convention is that a sentence changing truth value is recorded **in either direction**, and this is the both-directions staleness the file exists to catch. **Fix, and it is two halves with different owners. The half that needs no decision: add a B-218 entry to the comment log recording that the CI sentence was false when written, is now true, and why. The half that is blocked on D-115: give `LAST_REVIEWED` a bump trigger that is not per-item — a periodic re-verification of the "Where we fall short" list, owned by a recurring row, with the date moving only then.** Note for whoever builds B-244, B-245, B-247 and B-249: those are customer surfaces, so when they ship it is the "Where we fall short" list that changes, **not** `LAST_REVIEWED` | PRD 01 §6.8; D-115; B-218 | S | B-218 | Phase 2 |
| 83byn | B-251 | **The current month on the management pack is a 1.09:1 tint plus a font-weight bump** (accessibility review finding 9, 2026-09-01; **verified against the code by token arithmetic; the rendered appearance needs confirmation at build time**; **SC 1.4.11 Non-text Contrast (AA)**). `apps/web/app/admin/reports/pack/page.tsx:107-125`, same pattern at `apps/web/components/admin/side-nav.tsx:99`, `units-subnav.tsx` and `apps/web/app/admin/tenants/page.tsx:100`. `bg-accent` is `oklch(0.97 0 0)` against `--background: oklch(1 0 0)` — **1.09:1** — and the selected month in the "Other months" nav is distinguished by that tint plus `font-medium` against the default weight. **1.4.1 Use of Colour is met** (weight is a non-colour signal) and `aria-current="page"` at `:114` means assistive technology is told correctly, so **this is a sighted low-vision problem specifically**: twelve month chips of which one is "current" at a 500-vs-400 weight difference at 14px is not a state a user with reduced contrast sensitivity can pick out. **Fix: give the current chip a `border-2` in `--input` (already 3:1 by construction, `globals.css:76`) or `--foreground`, keeping `bg-accent`; one shared class, three call sites, `side-nav.tsx:99` included** | PRD 02 §5.5; PRD 01 §6.8; B-220 | S | B-220 | Phase 2 |
| 84 | B-084 ✅ | **Split into four parts, 2026-08-18** (owner), because this row is four deliverables and two of them are correctness-critical — and because a management pack or an accounting export cut from LIVE data silently disagrees with itself between runs, so the frozen close has to land first and the other two read from it. **Part 1 ✅ (monthly close + frozen snapshots)**: `AccountingPeriod` per facility per month, filed through `/admin/reports/close` under a new `accounting:close` permission held at regional and above — closing fixes the figures a site is measured on, which is why it stops short of `manager` for the same reason `auctions:approve` does. **D-65 settles what "frozen" means**, and it is the whole feature: unit occupancy reads `Unit.status` which this system keeps no history of, and `delinquencyReport` takes no date parameter at all, so both answer "as of now" whatever range they are handed — a July figure is observable only in July and the filed copy is the only record there will ever be. Those are frozen and never drift-checked. Billed, collected, discounts, write-offs, refunds, unapplied, economic occupancy and move counts come from dated rows, so they are frozen AND re-run on view, with any difference reported as drift — a voided invoice, a backdated adjustment, a corrected move-out. Conflating the two fails both ways and the split is enforced by types, not discipline. Month bounds come from the same `monthBounds` a tenant statement uses, so a statement and a close for one month cover exactly the same days; the resolved window is STORED, so a later timezone correction cannot re-slice a filed month. A month cannot be closed before it has ended in the facility's own timezone. Reopening requires a reason, clears the snapshot rather than leaving withdrawn figures on display, and writes them to the audit log, which is the only place they survive. **D-64: the management pack is HTML, not the PDF US-40 names** — B-023's finding that no library here emits *tagged* PDFs still stands, and this is the second place PRD text asks for one. **Part 2 ✅ (QuickBooks journal export)**: `/admin/reports/journal.csv` cuts a balanced general-journal entry from a CLOSED month's frozen figures — refusing an open one, which is the ordering argument from the split made enforceable. **D-66**: ten named accounts configured per facility on the close screen, matched by NAME because that is what QuickBooks Online's import matches on, with a conventional chart as the per-field fallback so an operator who never opens the form still gets a file that imports. `buildJournal` asserts its own balance and throws otherwise — unreachable by construction, checked anyway, because an unbalanced journal reaching a real general ledger is invisible from here; a swept test covers 100+ combinations. **Two omissions that look like bugs and are the point:** refunds are NOT journaled, because a refund unwinds its original payment's allocations so collected is already net of it and a second entry would take the money out twice **while balancing**; and referral rewards are taken OUT of the discount figure rather than posted beside it, because they are a subset of it — the same invoice line type split by description. Both are asserted by name so a later reader does not "fix" them. The snapshot gained a category split (version 2), because a journal credits rental income, fee income and sales tax payable to different accounts and a single total cannot be taken apart afterwards; a version-1 snapshot is **refused with an instruction to reopen and re-close**, never exported with a guessed split. Drift now covers the category leaves too, so a rent-to-fee reclassification that leaves the total unchanged is reported. **Part 3 ✅ (scheduled report emails)**: `ReportSubscription` per facility — a report, a cadence and a recipient list — sent by a `reports.email` job at 6am facility-local, after the overnight billing and delinquency sweeps so the figures already include last night's invoices and late fees. **This is the first generated email in the product, so it is the first to meet PRD 05 FR-9a, and every clause of it is now a test rather than a review note**: `lang` on the root, a real `<h1>` then `<h2>`s in order with nothing skipped, `<th scope>` on columns AND rows, a caption on every table, no images at all (which settles the alt-text and text-as-image clauses together), link text that names its destination, no colour anywhere to carry meaning, and a text part built from the same document rather than by stripping tags — asserted by checking it contains no markup and still carries every figure. **D-67 settles two things.** There is **no `lastSentAt` column**: the send is keyed `report:{subscription}:{period}:{recipient}` and `sendDirectEmail` already refuses a repeat, so a second record of the same fact is a second thing that can disagree with it. And the job does **not** invent an actor — `facilityAccess` correctly gives the system actor nothing, so the report layer gained facility-explicit variants (`occupancyForFacility`, `facilityRevenue`, `agingForFacility`, `movesForFacility`) called after authorization already happened at subscribe time; widening `facilityAccess` for system would have made every facility-scoped query permissive for background work. That extraction also removed the waste part 1's own comment recorded. **A monthly report says whether its month has been closed**, which is the link that makes part 1 pay for itself: an email showing numbers that quietly differ from the filed ones is exactly the confusion the close exists to remove. A bad recipient address is refused rather than dropped. **Found the hard way, and now guarded:** the scheduled job was never registered — every other piece was built and tested and none of it could have run, and both a full unit suite and a green e2e sweep passed over it. A test now asserts the registration itself. **Part 4 ✅ (management summary pack)**: `/admin/reports/pack` — the whole month on one page, in the five questions an owner actually asks (how full it was, what it earned, what it gave away or lost, what was owed, who came and went). **HTML, not the monthly PDF US-40 names** (D-64). **Built as an `EmailDocument`**, the structure part 3 defined, which is reuse with a reason rather than convenience: a pack an owner OPENS and a pack an owner is SENT must not be able to say different things about one month, so there is one document and two renderings — and it is subscribable through part 3, monthly only, because a weekly pack would have nothing filed to read and would quietly send live figures. **It reads the FILED figures when the month is closed**, which is the payoff of the whole four-part ordering: a pack cut live changes between the day it is read and the day it is quoted. **The provenance sentence leads the page**, never trails it, and has three genuinely different forms — not closed and can still change; closed and settled; closed but N named figures no longer match, which it calls a restatement to explain rather than an error. Those numbers get quoted in a board meeting and "can this change?" is what decides whether they should be. Promotional discounts are separated from referral rewards (the same reasoning as D-66 and the revenue report), tax is labelled as held for the state rather than as income, refunds are labelled as already deducted so nobody subtracts them twice, and the over-90 bucket carries the words "needs attention" rather than a colour. **Finished converting `figuresFor` to be facility-explicit**, which is where part 1's own comment about computing every facility and throwing most of it away stops being true. **B-084 is complete.** Reporting depth: scheduled report emails, monthly close with frozen snapshots, management summary pack, QuickBooks-compatible journal export. **This is the first item that generates email HTML nobody wrote by hand, so it is the first that has to meet PRD 05 FR-9a** (added 2026-08-12 from the accessibility review, which found no email criteria anywhere in PRD 05): a real text alternative part, `lang`, actual headings in order, `<th scope>` on every tabular figure, nothing carried by colour alone, no text rendered as an image, and link text that names its destination. Cheap here, expensive once templates exist, are versioned, and have rendered snapshots stored against thousands of message rows | PRD 02 US-40, §8; PRD 05 FR-9a | M | B-055, B-042 | Phase 2 |

## Milestone 8: Phase 3 — grow & extend

| # | ID | Item | PRD/Feature | Size | Depends on | Phase |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 83c | B-132 ✅ | **The demo leases that were delinquent in name only now owe real money** (found 2026-08-18 while fixing B-130; built 2026-08-19). Three of the four leases carrying a delinquency label had **no invoices and no ledger entries whatsoever** — only the primary facility's `delinquent` lease, the one B-114 and B-130 had already worked on, had any financial state. So every multi-facility aging figure was one lease at one site wearing three hats, and a `pending_auction` lease that owes nothing can never have a served lien notice behind it, which is the auction pipeline's own scheduling precondition (B-062) — the auction arc could not be walked end to end, for exactly the reason the notice arc could not before B-130. **Fixed in B-130's shape, extracted as `seedUnpaidRent`:** the INVOICE first, the ledger charge carrying its `invoiceId`, because `reconcile` counts invoices-outstanding PLUS charges no invoice accounts for and an unlinked charge is therefore counted twice. One month for each `delinquent` lease, **three** for each `pending_auction` one, oldest first and a month apart, since every aging figure reads `daysPastDue` from the oldest unpaid invoice's original due date (D-25). **Measured with the product's own `previewNotice`, not a re-derivation:** lien-notice eligibility went **1 of 14 → 2 of 14**, being Austin's `delinquent` and `pending_auction` leases. **Dallas's two now refuse with `no_template` rather than `nothing_owed`, and that is left as it is on purpose** — B-061's rule is that a facility which has not written its notice text generates nothing instead of mailing the unedited example, so the demo now carries one configured facility and one that is not, which demonstrates the guard better than two identical sites would. **The auction CASE is seeded too**, through the product's own `openAuctionCase` (which writes no audit entry, so the script's contract holds): `flag_auction_eligible` opens one on the way to `pending_auction`, so that status with no case behind it is state the product itself cannot reach. `AuctionCase.lease` and `.unit` are `onDelete: Restrict`, so the teardown gained a delete or the NEXT re-seed would fail on a foreign key rather than reset. **Deliberately NOT seeded: a generated or served notice, and any advertising record** — both are claims about documents served and advertisements run, which is what D-63 refused for this exact arc; the demo leaves them walkable through the product. **Left behind:** `/admin/auctions/{id}` still has no axe scan, and there is now a real case id to scan against for the first time; and nothing walks the auction arc end to end yet, because generating and serving a notice against shared demo data needs B-120's scoped-fixture discipline rather than a tail-end edit | PRD 02 §4.6 US-28; B-062, B-130 | S | B-130 | Phase 2 |
| 84a | B-131 ✅ | **Unit occupancy is now historical, and says which instant it answers for when it cannot be** (found 2026-08-18 while building B-084 part 1; built 2026-08-19 — **D-68**). `occupancyForFacility` took `periodStart`/`periodEnd` and used them for the collected figure and the street rate while reading `unit.status` with **no date filter at all**, so asking for July's occupancy in December returned December's unit statuses under July's heading — on a screen with a date picker on it. **`UnitStatusHistory`, written by a database trigger, not by application code.** `Unit.status` is derived and has two application writers (`recomputeUnitStatus`, and the bulk status operation's pre-evaluated `to`) plus two seeds; a history that depends on each of them remembering is a history with holes, and a hole here is **indistinguishable from "the status did not change"** — the read cannot tell them apart, so a partial history is not a weaker version of this, it is a wrong one. The trigger covers psql, future migrations and callers nobody has written; one test inserts a unit and asserts a row appeared without the application asking. It fires on `AFTER UPDATE OF status` and returns early on a same-value write, or every unrelated unit edit touching the column would log a change that did not happen. **The gap is stated, never papered over.** History begins at the migration, which backfilled one row per existing unit. `audit_log` was considered as a backfill source and rejected: it carries only the HUMAN status changes, never the derived recompute that moves a unit to `occupied` on move-in, so a reconstruction would be confidently partial — and a wrong past presented as recorded is worse than an honest gap, because nothing downstream can detect it. So a period older than the history returns TODAY'S figures flagged `followsPeriod: false` with a `reason`, and every surface prints **one shared sentence** — the screen, the scheduled email, and the CSV as an `as at` date column — because a reader who sees two wordings of the same caveat trusts neither. Four reasons are carried rather than re-derived from a bare false (`as-at-period-end`, `period-not-ended`, `before-history`, `no-units`); "the month is older than the history" and "the month has not finished" are different sentences to a reader. A unit absent from the as-at map did not exist yet and drops out of **both** numerator and denominator, so a building opened in August is correctly not in July's denominator; economic occupancy is measured over the same unit set, because the metrics module exists to stop two figures on one page disagreeing about which units count. The portfolio total makes the **weakest** claim any facility makes, and facilities with no units are dropped rather than allowed to drag it false forever. **D-65's freeze is deliberately NOT reopened** — occupancy is reproducible only for months after the history begins, so drift-checking filed months would report drift on every one filed before it, forever; that is a later item's decision, not a side effect of this one. **Left behind:** unit TYPE is not historised, so a retyped or resized unit carries today's square footage into a past month; and the management pack still reads filed point-in-time figures, so its provenance sentence is unchanged (adding an as-at field there is a snapshot version bump, which D-66 already makes expensive) | PRD 02 US-39.1, §8; D-65; D-68; B-084 | M | B-042 | Phase 2 |
| 85 | B-085 | First real gate-vendor driver (OpenTech CIA or PTI StorLogix Cloud — needs partner agreement, PRD 03 OQ-5) + kiosk-mode evaluation/decision. **Accessibility acceptance criteria added 2026-08-24** (accessibility review — an at-risk row rather than a new one, because the row exists and its criteria do not): a kiosk is a fixed-viewport touch surface with a session timeout, which is **SC 2.2.1 Timing Adjustable (A)** in its purest form, and PRD 03 defaults kiosk mode to "no" with no accessibility wording either way. **If the evaluation lands on yes**, the kiosk surface ships with a session timeout that warns at 20 seconds remaining and offers a one-press extension (2.2.1), a rental flow completable end to end with an external keyboard (2.1.1 A) including a visible focus indicator at the kiosk's actual contrast, reflow at the kiosk's real pixel width with no horizontal scrolling (**1.4.10 AA**), and error suggestion on every refused field (**3.3.2 A**, **3.3.3 AA**) since a walk-up user has no support channel on the screen. **If it lands on no**, the evaluation records "no" with its reasoning and the row ships **no kiosk surface at all** — a half-built kiosk is the one shape these criteria cannot be retrofitted onto. Depends on the OQ-5/D-3 decision either way | PRD 00 §6 Phase 2 (hardware); PRD 03 §8 Phase 2–3; PRD 01 §6.8.1; D-3 | L | B-080 | Phase 3 |
| 86 | B-086 | **Split into two parts, 2026-08-25**, because most of US-8 turned out to be built already and what was left divides cleanly at the hardware boundary. **Part 1 ✅ — time-boxing and the scope that was never enforced** (built 2026-08-25 — **D-100**, **D-101**). US-8's AC2 (individual revocation, delinquency cascade) and AC3 (per-person event attribution) were shipped by US-9/B-029 and B-105 and needed nothing; AC1's *scope* was the genuine gap. `AuthorizedAccessPerson.expiresAt` plus `access.expire-shared` (hour 0 facility-local, the `reservation.expire` shape) is the time-boxing, and it **revokes through the outbox rather than filtering our own reads** — a keypad decides from the codes it was last told about, so a row we merely hide still opens the gate. It also fixed a real latent bug: **`accessHours` had been written since B-029 and read by nothing**, so a manager who set "weekends only" at the counter had configured precisely nothing — it now reaches the controller through `scheduleForGrant`, NARROWED against the facility's own gate hours (`narrowSchedule`), because a per-person window that could widen is `extendedHours` — a paid add-on — obtained by typing 00:00–23:59 into a portal form (**D-100**). Portal controls for both, since a column that configures behaviour gets its control in the same item; the schedule ships as **three presets, not a weekly-schedule editor** (**D-101**). **Part 2 — the unlock control and OQ-2, still to build**: `AccessCredentialType.mobile_key` (declared since B-002, unused), simulated Bluetooth unlock, and the native-app/PWA spike. **Accessibility acceptance criteria added 2026-08-24, and they belong to part 2** (accessibility review — an at-risk row rather than a new one; the default build of a Bluetooth unlock is an icon button that changes colour plus a toast, which fails four SCs at once): the unlock control exposes its state programmatically via `aria-pressed`/`aria-busy` (**4.1.2 A**) and announces the outcome from a live region **present before the press** (**4.1.3 AA**), never by colour or icon alone (**1.4.1 A**); a time-boxed credential states its remaining validity as an **absolute facility-local time**, per the standing rule at PRD 01 §6.8.1 that B-142 applied to the transfer hold, never as a countdown a screen-reader user has to poll (**2.2.1 A**) — **met by part 1** for the expiry date, which `/portal/access` renders as an absolute facility-local day; and a failed unlock names the facility's phone number in text rather than presenting a retry icon, because the failure state is where a tenant is standing outside a gate. | 88 | B-088 ✅ | **Split into two parts, 2026-08-19**, per note 8's rule that bundled Phase 2/3 rows are split when they are reached: the two halves share no screen, no data and no PRD section. **Part 1 ✅ — revenue-management aids** (built 2026-08-19 — **D-74**). `/admin/units/rates`, a Rates tab beside Types: per-unit-type occupancy, a two-band suggestion ladder from US-12's own 92% example, and one-click apply that is **pre-filled but editable** — the rule proposes and the person prices. The rule is pure and lives in `packages/core/pricing` beside `rate-increase.ts`, because the rate a NEW tenant is quoted and the rate an EXISTING tenant is raised to are different prices with different consequences and must never be confused; nothing here reads or writes `Lease.monthlyRateCents`, and the screen says so in its first sentence with a link to `/admin/rate-increases`. Occupancy comes from the metrics module per unit type rather than being recomputed (D-25), which a test pins by exercising the two judgement calls — `maintenance` is rentable, `unrentable` is not. **Increases only, never a cut, plus three guards (D-74):** a 5-unit floor, a 90-day cooldown, and silence when a change is already scheduled. The cooldown is the one that matters — occupancy does not fall the day a rate rises, so without it "one-click apply" compounds +8% weekly into a 50% rise in a quarter. Applying reuses `publishUnitTypeRate`, so the permission, the audit entry and the effective-dating are the existing ones rather than a second write path. **Part 2 ✅ — owner KPI dashboard** (built 2026-08-19 — **D-75**). `/admin/reports/kpi`: the last 12 months of unit occupancy, economic occupancy, collected, receivables and net moves, portfolio-wide. **What it adds over the two existing surfaces is TIME** — `/admin` answers "how are we doing now" and every report answers "how did we do in that period"; neither answers "is it getting better", which is the only question an owner not running the counter asks. **Built from the FILED month-end snapshots and never from a recomputation (D-75)**, which is correctness before it is speed: D-65 settled that point-in-time figures cannot be observed again, so re-deriving a past month answers a different question under the same name — and it is also one query instead of the ~150 round trips twelve months across a portfolio would otherwise cost. Ratios are recomputed from components, never averaged (the `sumOccupancy` trap, asserted in both places it hides, including economic occupancy where the snapshot stores only a ratio and the type system cannot catch it). **A month nobody closed is named, never drawn as zero**, and a month where only some sites filed says so — one site not closing its books otherwise looks exactly like the portfolio falling off a cliff. Unlike `buildJournal` it does NOT refuse a v1 snapshot: the category split was v2's only addition and no KPI here reads it, so refusing would blank a year of history to guard a field nothing on the page uses. **Superseded scoping note:** the open question was what an owner view adds beyond B-042's dashboard and B-084's reports; the answer was trend, and the snapshots were already the right source | PRD 02 US-12; PRD 00 §6 Phase 3 | M | B-042, B-055 | Phase 3 |

The literal `size` segment keeps a dimension out of the facility page's slug namespace. **D-77 is the substance:** this is the page type that manufactures thin content, and B-082 part 6's duplicate detector now runs as a GATE before publication rather than a report after it — a page scoring ≥0.8 against a sibling size gets `noindex, follow` and is left out of the sitemap. Measured, not assumed: the seven standard sizes clear at 0.738, two sizes outside the guide catalogue score 0.940 and are both gated. **Do not build: offer A/B testing** — at 2–10 facilities no offer test reaches significance inside a quarter, so the result is noise that prices get changed on; set a rate, watch move-ins for 60 days, adjust. **Marketplace (SpareFoot-class) channel evaluation is not a backlog item** — it trades a chunk of first-year revenue for a tenant who was already searching for you, and it is a decision for a specific site that will not fill. Confirmed by the owner 2026-07-31, with the *integration* surface (feed out, attribution in, rate parity, channel source) carved out to B-082 so the plumbing exists if that decision is ever taken | PRD 00 §6 Phase 3; PRD 04 §7 Phase 3; D-77 | M | B-082 | Phase 3 |

They render, they serve visitors, they list real inventory, and they are never offered to an index, with nothing an operator can do about it. Form behind a `<details>` on each full size; a cron-tick sweep that notifies **as many people as there are free units, oldest first**, holds each claim 72 hours and then gives the next person a turn; a cancel link that is the only credential a non-tenant has; and `/admin/reports/waitlist`, which exists because a waitlist is the only demand signal this product has for inventory it does **not** have. An unrecognised inbound now emits `sms.inbound_received` carrying the words, raises an `inbound_sms_review` task at the tenant's own facility (**`high` priority** — the only type in this queue where a person is standing somewhere waiting), and answers the sender that a human has it, with **their own site's phone number** rather than the shared SMS line. The message is on the task card, truncated, so nobody opens a task to triage it (B-115's rule). **Where the words live is D-83**: the domain event, because `Message` is outbound by construction and `Task.proof` is completion evidence — and **one task per MESSAGE, not per tenant per day**, or `createTask`'s `(type, entityId, businessDate)` key would swallow a second question the same afternoon. **Still deliberately not an inbox** — no threading, no `direction` column, no second queue to staff. **Left behind:** a text from a number matching no current tenant records the event but raises no task, because `Task` requires a facility and this SMS number is site-wide, so `To` cannot supply one; the sender is given a number to call | PRD 05 §8 Phase 3; D-78; D-83; PRD 02 US-41 | S | B-074, B-095 | Phase 3 |

The target unit is **held with a `Reservation` marked `source = 'transfer'`** (D-82, no schema change), which bought exactly one exception in `previewTransfer`/`transferTargets` — a unit `reserved` for the same tenant reads available to THAT tenant and nobody else, or the request would block the transfer it exists to set up. | 90d | B-136 ✅ | **A transfer quote that nothing honours** — built 2026-08-20 (**D-84**). The hold locks the rate for as long as it lives; an expired hold re-quotes; a staff-initiated transfer with no hold is unchanged. (Found 2026-08-20 while building B-090 part 2, not by a review.) The portal shows a tenant what the swap costs, holds the unit, and stores the figure on the hold as `quotedRateCents` — and then `completeTransfer` **re-reads the current street rate** when staff commit, which is correct for a staff-initiated transfer and wrong for one the tenant was quoted. A rate rise between the ask and the completion charges them something other than the number they agreed to, silently, and the number they agreed to is sitting on the row. **Deliberately not fixed inside B-090 part 2**, because the fix is a policy question rather than a patch: does a request lock a rate, for how long, and does the lock survive the tenant rescheduling? Those are the same three questions a reservation's own quote raises, and D-7/B-126 answered them for reservations by anchoring expiry to the move-in date — the cheap first move here is probably the same shape, honouring `quotedRateCents` for as long as the hold lives and re-quoting after. **The trigger is a rate change landing between an ask and its completion**, which at a 2–10 facility operator is weeks apart at most, so this is small and real rather than theoretical | PRD 02 §4.3 US-14; D-82; D-7 | S | B-090b | Phase 3 |
| 90e | B-090c ✅ | **Delinquency payment plans and self-cure** (B-090 part 3, built 2026-08-26). The `payment_plan` `LeaseHold` type has halted dunning, late fees and access suspension since B-096, with none of what the parent row's own audit named missing: a schedule, amounts, breach detection, or anything a tenant could see. `PaymentPlan`/`PaymentPlanInstallment` are new, scoped through the lease (no `facilityId` of their own, same reasoning as `LeaseHold`); creating one (`delinquency:execute_step`, manager and above — a financial commitment, not a counter conversation) places the SAME hold through the existing `placeHold`, in one transaction with the schedule (`placeHold` gained an optional trailing `client` param so the two can't split). **No stored per-installment paid flag** — status is derived every read by allocating payments from `LedgerEntry` against the schedule oldest-first, the D-65 reasoning applied one level down. A new nightly job, `delinquency.payment-plan-breach` at hour 4 (after autopay settles the ledger, before the ladder and the timeline both read `onHold`), breaks a plan the first installment it misses and lifts the hold automatically (`systemLiftHold` — no actor, the same posture `restoreAccessIfSettled` already takes), or completes one once every installment is covered. Staff UI on the tenant profile (schedule, cancel, a fixed `MAX_INSTALLMENTS`-row builder); portal UI is the "self-cure" half PRD 01 §9 asks for — the dashboard card names the next installment and links to a new read-only `/portal/payment-plan`, where before this a tenant on a plan had no way to know it existed short of calling the office. **Self-cure was built as visibility, not tenant-initiated origination** — staff still set up every plan; a tenant-facing REQUEST flow is real, additional scope (a policy question, not a UI gap) and was not built here. Found and fixed along the way: opening the new builder disclosure on a long tenant profile page tripped axe's `elmPartiallyObscured` heuristic under the page's pre-existing `[contain:layout]` — checked by hand (`elementsFromPoint` at the flagged node: a clean stack, nothing actually overlapping) and added to the hand-checked exemption list rather than worked around. Full unit suite 3,761 passed / 8 skipped, e2e (`portal`, `admin-tenants`, `a11y`) 402 passed / 6 failed — all 6 a pre-existing `mobile-chrome`-only flake, confirmed by stashing this row's diff and reproducing identically against unmodified `main` | PRD 02 §4.6 US-25; PRD 01 §9; B-090, B-096 | L | B-090, B-096 | Phase 3 |

## Appended after v1.0 — internal tooling

Added 2026-07-30, after the original 90 were sequenced. Appended rather than inserted so the global numbering above stays stable. **These are not in top-to-bottom build order** — their dependencies (B-003/B-004/B-005) are already built, so they are buildable at any point; the Phase column is the recommendation, not a gate.

| # | ID | Item | PRD/Feature | Size | Depends on | Phase |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 91 | B-091 ✅ | **Split into two parts, 2026-08-19** (owner), and the split has one constraint behind it: **no commit may ship “a session can start” before “writes are blocked and the banner shows”**, so part 1 deliberately builds no actor swap and nothing renders as the subject. **Part 1 ✅ — the guard and the record.** `ImpersonationSession` + two nullable `AuditLog` columns (one migration, purely additive so the append-only triggers are untouched, FR-26), the four permissions owner-only (D-13b), the escalation guard as ONE function with its adversarial suite (SR-1), the session lifecycle — start with a required reason, throttled, end, server-enforced expiry, and FR-9 re-validation that ends a session when the guard stops agreeing — and dual attribution plumbed through `recordAudit`. **Part 2 ✅ — enforcement, banner, UI** (built 2026-08-19 — **D-69**, **D-70**). The actor swap lives in ONE function, `currentActor()`, because every screen and service in the app already resolves who is asking through it — so an impersonated request runs as the subject everywhere at once rather than in the places somebody remembered. Read-only is enforced **by HTTP method at the edge**, not by a list of blocked actions: every non-GET is refused while the session cookie is present, with two exempt paths (the way out, and sign-out). That is what makes SR-2 hold for the 53 files of server actions in this repo and for screens nobody has written yet, and it is why **FR-12's hard-block list is deliberately NOT built as a list** — with `impersonation:write` unshipped the rule is "block everything", and a classification that cannot be exercised cannot be trusted when it finally is (D-70). **The one member of that list which is not a mutation IS built:** an impersonated portal renders no gate code, dropped from the data rather than hidden in the markup, because PRD 03 SR-2 makes revealing one a separate audited permission and no write block catches a GET. The session id travels in its own httpOnly cookie rather than as a JWT claim (**D-69**) — the row is the authority either way, and a forged cookie fails the binding check while still tripping the write block, so it can only ever reduce access. Banner first in the document ahead of the skip link (FR-14/FR-15), start controls with required reason on the tenant profile and the staff-security screen, "Return to my account" as a route handler (a server action POSTs to its own page and so has no path to exempt), and **PRD 09 OQ-1's disclosure drafted into the public privacy notice**, saying in as many words that we do not notify you — the half that makes D-13a defensible rather than merely undisclosed. `impersonation:write` is in neither part — PRD 09 §8 lists it as "later / optional" and OQ-2 asks whether it should ship at all. **Left behind:** a blocked submit returns a 403 rather than a message beside the field (D-70 records the two graceful alternatives and why both were refused), and B-092 owns the oversight half that FR-21 calls load-bearing | PRD 09 §5–7 Phase A | L | B-003, B-004, B-005 | Phase 2 |
| 92 | B-092 ✅ | **Impersonation oversight** (built 2026-08-19 — **D-71**, **D-72**, **D-73**). `/admin/impersonation`, owner-only: sessions running right now with a one-click force-end, the filterable record with a matching CSV, and FR-20's frequency flags. **No tenant notification and no tenant-facing activity view** (D-13a) — which makes this the *only* misuse-detection channel, so it should not trail B-091 indefinitely. **The active list filters `expiresAt > now` AS WELL AS `endedAt IS NULL`**, which is the rule B-091 part 1 wrote down and told this item not to drop: expiry is enforced lazily, so a session whose impersonator closed their laptop is unexpired on paper and expired in fact, and listing it would offer a force-end button that ends nothing. It has its own test. **Force-end authorises by "you can only end what you can see"** — the id must be in `activeSessions(actor)` — so widening `impersonation:oversee` to a regional stays a seed change and confines them to their own sites without this function changing. **FR-19 names PRD 02 US-38's audit-log surface as the thing to mirror and that surface does not exist** — `findAuditEntries` has had no consumer outside tests since B-005 — so this follows the `/admin/reports` convention instead: filters in the query string, a `.csv` sibling re-reading the same string through the same function. **"Filterable by facility" resolves through the SUBJECT** (D-72), because the session row deliberately has no facility and the owner's question is whose account was opened, not what the impersonator could have reached. **FR-20's threshold is a named constant of 5 distinct subjects a day, not a column** (D-73) — OQ-3 wants a real N and there is no observed usage to tune against. **PRD 09 OQ-2 is answered: `impersonation:write` is DELETED** (D-71, owner) rather than deferred — a permission an owner can grant that changes nothing is a promise the product does not keep. That exposed a real gap: the seed pruned stale role GRANTS but never removed a retired `Permission` row, so `rbac-db.test.ts`'s catalog check failed against every database seeded before the removal; the seed now prunes retired permissions and says how many. **Left behind:** an owner cannot yet see WHAT was looked at during a session, only that it happened — read-only means no audit entries are written during one, so there is nothing to show until write mode exists (which D-71 just decided against) | PRD 09 §5.5, §8 Phase B | M | B-091 | Phase 2 |

---

## How to use this backlog with Claude Code

- **Work strictly top to bottom.** The order encodes the dependency graph; when you reach an item, everything it depends on is already built. Don't cherry-pick a later item unless its entire "Depends on" chain is done.
- **One item (or one small cluster) per session.** S/M items are a single focused session. L items should be split into 2–3 sessions at start ("build the state machine," then "build the UI on it"). B-090 (XL) must be split before starting.
- **Load the source PRD section as context.** Each item's PRD/Feature column points at the authoritative spec (e.g., "PRD 02 §4.5 US-17/US-18" → open `02-admin-dashboard-prd.md` and paste/reference that section plus the master PRD's data model §7.5). The PRD's acceptance criteria are the item's definition of done.
- **Accessibility is loaded, not remembered.** Every customer-facing item additionally loads **PRD 01 §6.8 and §6.8.1** (the per-flow acceptance criteria); every admin item additionally loads **PRD 02 §5.5**. This is not a formality — the accessibility audit of 2026-07-31 found that the shipped admin routes carried most of the blocking findings precisely because PRD 02 contained no accessibility text at all, so every admin session had built from a spec that never mentioned AA. Customer-facing items also load **PRD 01 §6.10** (the D-15 lexicon).
- **Start each session with:** the backlog row, the PRD section(s), the master PRD's cross-cutting requirements (§7 — auth, WCAG 2.1 AA, mobile-first, PCI scope, data model), and the current schema. End each session with tests passing (billing math and the rental funnel are the two places the PRDs demand tests) and the seed script updated if entities changed.
- **Two demo checkpoints:** after B-032 run the golden-path-1 demo (search → rent → pay → simulated gate code → welcome email). After B-055 run golden-path-2 (nightly run invoices a seeded lease → due-soon reminder → simulated failed payment → dunning step 1 → magic-link payment halts the ladder). ✅ Run and kept: `tests/golden-path-2-db.test.ts` drives the real `SCHEDULED_JOBS` registry night by night, so the checkpoint fails a pull request rather than being a thing that passed once.
- **Don't resolve flagged conflicts silently.** If an item touches something in the Flagged gaps/conflicts list, get an owner decision first or build to the master PRD's position and note it.

## Rationale: top ordering decisions & trade-offs

1. **Foundation before any feature (B-001–B-012).** Every PRD reads/writes the shared schema (master §7.5) and assumes facility scoping, RBAC, audit logging, and an event bus. Building these once, first, prevents each module re-inventing them — the master PRD explicitly forbids modules re-deciding cross-cutting concerns.
2. **Golden Path 1 before the tenant portal or billing.** The MVP exit criterion (master §6) is "a real prospect can find a facility, rent and pay online, and appear in the rent roll." Milestone 2 is the shortest dependency chain to that: it forces the checkout state machine, Stripe, document generation, the ACS + simulator, and the comms core into existence — the five hardest shared subsystems — while producing a demoable product early.
3. **ACS + simulator inside Milestone 2, real vendors last.** PRD 03's own conclusion: the market is partner-gated, so the simulated adapter *is* the primary adapter for this build and the contract fixture for future real drivers. Gate codes are therefore "simulated but real" from the first move-in; vendor drivers wait until Phase 3 (B-085), matching PRD 03 over the master's more optimistic Phase 2 wording (flagged below).
4. **Comms core lands in Milestone 2, not 4.** The move-in confirmation email is required for Golden Path 1 anyway, so the idempotent send pipeline, message log, and Resend integration get built early and Milestone 4 only adds rules/templates on top. SMS consent capture and 10DLC registration also start here because registration has unknown external latency (PRD 05 §6.3) — consent must exist before SMS does.
5. **Portal before the autopay run.** B-045 (autopay) needs saved payment methods and the autopay toggle (B-036); the portal milestone also gives dunning links somewhere to land (B-051 deep-links into the pay screen). Interleaving admin essentials (tenant profile, POS, move-out) here means staff can operate the facility as soon as tenants exist.
6. **Billing before delinquency, delinquency before lien.** The dunning ladder (B-052) is driven by billing-engine day events by explicit PRD 05 requirement, so invoices/late fees must exist first. The full delinquency→lien→auction pipeline follows in Milestone 5 as Phase 2 per the master roadmap, even though PRD 02 labels it MVP (flagged) — the learning value and legal risk both argue for doing it after the money loop works.
7. **Marketing/SEO after the transactional core.** SEO pages sell inventory; there must be inventory, live pricing, and a working rental funnel to sell into. Within Milestone 6, master-MVP items (SEO infrastructure, lead capture, promotions) precede master-Phase-2 items (reviews, drips, abandonment). The main trade-off: promotions (B-070) sit late despite touching checkout — acceptable because checkout works without discounts, and B-070 depends on billing (B-044) to apply them to invoices correctly.
8. **Deliberate bundling at the tail.** Phase 2/3 items are bundled (B-078, B-080–B-082, B-089–B-090) to keep the list at ~90; split them when they're reached. MVP items are granular because they'll actually be built next.
9. **The five items added on 2026-07-31, and why they sit where they sit.**
   - **B-093 and B-094 (remediation) go next, before B-017.** They are fixes to already-shipped code — two design tokens, a keyboard-hostile input, customer-facing copy showing an internal ticket number, an accessibility statement making four claims the build cannot support, a missing skip link, a form layer that surfaces no errors at all. None of it is large. All of it gets multiplied by the next item: B-017 alone adds filters, a size guide and a cost summary to the public site, and every one of them inherits the same tokens and the same status-region pattern. Work of this shape never gets done unless it has a number, which is why it has one.
   - **B-095 (`Task`) goes before B-026** because B-026 is the first consumer — "downstream failures become admin tasks, never dead ends" needs somewhere to put them — and because five later items each specify their own staff queue against a schema that already names `Task` and never builds it. Building it once, first, is the same argument that put RBAC and the audit log in Milestone 1.
   - **B-096 (lease holds) goes right after B-038** so it has a profile to render on, and comfortably before B-052, which already says the dunning ladder halts on a "hold" that nothing defines.
   - **B-098 (access suspension) goes after B-047**, once late fees exist and the shared `daysPastDue()` from B-046 is in place. It is the one place MVP acquires actual collections leverage, and it deliberately reuses the ACS built at B-027 rather than waiting for the Phase-2 delinquency engine, which then inherits it as a configured step. D-1 is untouched: only the threshold moved.
   - **B-097 (phone/counter capture) goes after B-039**, once the reservation service and counter operations both exist. It is the only MVP item that captures the demand arriving by phone; without it the conversion reporting built later will show web leads only, and look excellent.
   - **Four schema additions are folded into existing items rather than given their own** — `LeaseRateChange` into B-026, address-of-record history into B-037/B-038, `Payment.receivedByStaffId` into B-039, and the `notice_email` consent value into B-032. Each is a few columns whose entire value is that they exist *before* the data does: none can be reconstructed retroactively, and each has a workflow item downstream (B-076, B-061, B-078) that is unbuildable without the history it was supposed to have been keeping.

10. **The eleven items added on 2026-08-12 (B-109–B-119), and why they sit between B-105 and B-106.** They are the second review pass — UX and accessibility over everything shipped through B-105 — and every one is a defect in built code rather than a new feature, so they belong at the build frontier and ahead of the two remaining feature rows in that block. Three of them (**B-110, B-111, B-112**) are hard prerequisites of **B-106** and now appear in its "Depends on": B-106 rewrites the checkout core for multi-unit, and a checkout that cannot go backwards, never states why a total moved, asks fourteen questions on step 1 and announces nothing on a step change would have each of those reproduced once per unit. That is the same argument that put B-093/B-094 before B-017, and it is the reason the position is a constraint rather than a preference. **B-119 (the scan contract) is deliberately last** in the block: it asserts what B-110 fixes and loops reflow over the admin routes B-116 fixes, so running it earlier only produces a suite that is red for known reasons. **B-109 is first** because it is the cheapest and one of its three stale sentences is actively costing money — the counter is told there is no promotions engine while the website prices the promotion, so a phone quote and a web quote for the same unit disagree. Two findings were merged rather than numbered: the MFA recovery-code save path and the sign-in form's second-factor field went into **B-108**, which already opens both files.

11. **B-120 (e2e repeatability) sits with B-119 rather than with the review block.** It came out of building B-110, not out of the 2026-08-12 reviews, and it is here for the same reason B-119 is last: both are about whether a red suite means anything. B-119 makes the scans cover what they claim to; B-120 makes a failure attributable. Neither is a feature, and either one skipped leaves the next six items being verified by a suite that cannot distinguish a regression from a stale fixture or a stray dev server on port 3000.

12. **The twenty items added on 2026-08-21 (B-137–B-156), and why they sit ahead of every open row.** They are the third review pass — operator, UX and accessibility over everything shipped in **B-120 through B-136** — and, as with the 2026-08-12 block, every one is a defect in built code or an AC no row owns rather than a new feature, so they belong at the build frontier. They are inserted immediately after **B-130 (83b)**, which puts them ahead of all five rows open when the block was written (**B-129, B-085, B-086, B-133, B-134**). That position is a constraint rather than a preference: every one of those five is blocked on a partner agreement, an API credential or a real portfolio, none of which this project controls, so leaving twenty confirmed defects behind them means the frontier stops moving.
    - **B-137/B-138 first**, because the transfer defect is the only one in the block that loses an operator money it cannot get back: collections stop entirely on a live tenant, and the ladder runs on an active-duty servicemember whose hold did not survive the transfer. It is a split, and **the split carries B-091's constraint** — B-137 (holds carry over, lien-pipeline escape closed) must land before B-138 (collections resume), because resuming the ladder first runs it against exactly the tenants whose holds have not yet been carried.
    - **B-139 next**, because CLAUDE.md makes the accessibility statement a standing obligation, the page is currently false in the overstating direction, and the fix is an hour.
    - **B-140 next** as the cheapest thing actively reaching tenants — a transfer hold mailing an existing tenant a move-in link D-82 ensured never exists.
    - **B-141 and B-142 next**, the two silent failures: a queue whose Complete button is indistinguishable from a broken one, and a request screen that re-renders byte-identical on refusal. **B-142 ranks above its size** because two reviewers found it independently and a correct sibling already exists in the codebase.
    - **B-156 is deliberately last**, exactly as B-119 was in the previous block: it asserts what B-141 and B-142 fix.
    - **Eight findings were merged rather than numbered.** The transfer-date ceiling, the unstated hold expiry, the raw `×` in a radio's accessible name and the invisible pending request all went into **B-142** (one screen, one session); the occupancy table's missing row headers into **B-150** (same file, same size); the unscanned admin transfer wizard, B-122's never-opened promo disclosure and the three structural test-coverage gaps into **B-156**. The unread `lien` fee type went into **B-146** rather than becoming its own row, and **no new task type** was created for returned payments — the existing `failed_payment` queue carries them, per US-41.
    - **Four questions were not answered here** and are appended to `07-decisions.md` as **D-85** (may a lien-pipeline lease transfer at all, and under whose authority), **D-86** (do arrears follow the tenant or the lease), **D-87** (does a waitlist notification hold the unit) and **D-88** (what happens to a rate increase whose notice bounced). B-138, B-152 and B-154 are each written to whichever answer their decision takes.

13. **The twenty-six items added on 2026-08-24 (B-159–B-184), and why they too sit ahead of every open row.** They are the fourth review pass — operator, UX and accessibility over everything shipped in **B-137 through B-158** — and the criterion is the one the 2026-08-12 and 2026-08-21 blocks used: every one is a verified defect in built code or an acceptance criterion no row owns, not a new feature, so they belong at the build frontier. They are inserted immediately after **B-157/B-158 (`83bu`/`83bv`)**, which puts them ahead of all five rows still open (**B-129, B-085, B-086, B-133, B-134**) — and that position is the same constraint as last time rather than a preference: every one of those five is blocked on a partner agreement, an API credential or a real portfolio, none of which this project controls, so leaving twenty-six confirmed defects behind them means the frontier stops moving.
    - **B-159 is first, ahead of larger and more expensive defects**, because it is not a defect a user has to reach — it is a live public claim about this codebase, and CLAUDE.md makes that page a standing obligation. It has now been false in the overstating direction twice in twelve days; the correction is prose and an hour, and a page claiming that failing scans block a release while Vercel ships independently of Actions is the one item here that converts a fixable bug into an alleged misrepresentation.
    - **B-160 is the largest single item in the block and is deliberately merged into nothing.** A D-85 lien transfer leaves the auction file naming the unit the notice was served on: staff cut the lock on a re-rented unit, and after a completed sale the proceeds post to the lease B-138 zeroed while the live lease still shows full arrears and the ladder keeps running. It is money, a stranger's goods and a §59 record in one defect.
    - **B-161 next**, because a returned ACH on an old invoice replays the whole ladder in one night — four letters, re-assessed late fees, access cut and an auction case opened by a bank reversal with no human in it. Both B-146 and B-147 named it unclosed; nothing picked it up.
    - **B-162–B-164 are the rest of the transfer cluster**, in the order the data flows: pricing and the ECRI clock, then the insurance proof the transfer silently stops monitoring, then the portal move-out hole that is B-137's fix one file over. B-164 is small and late in the cluster only because it is the same fix already written down.
    - **B-165 before B-177**, because the batch preview B-177 redesigns has to have a rule to display; a screen that states the rule before the rule exists states the default hardcoded constant.
    - **B-170 sits ahead of the four rows that use its mechanism** (B-176, B-178, B-181, and B-184's manual passes). It builds the page-level announcement region that survives the row it reports on; every other admin row in this block that removes its own card depends on it existing.
    - **B-184 is deliberately last**, exactly as B-119 and B-156 were in the previous two blocks: it asserts what B-170, B-171, B-172, B-176, B-177 and B-183 fix, so running it earlier only produces a suite red for reasons already written down. **T6's three manual passes are part of its definition of done, with the owner each finding names** — the VoiceOver money-path pass and the keyboard pass over the task queues belong to whoever takes B-170, in that session, because a text-only session cannot verify that its fix is success rather than silence; the live-map pass belongs to the first session with a billed vendor key. A pass not recorded in `PROGRESS.md` did not happen, which is the rule B-159 is correcting the public page against.
    - **Two findings became acceptance criteria on existing rows rather than new rows.** The accessibility review's two at-risk items are appended in place to **B-085** (kiosk mode: 2.2.1, 1.4.10, 3.3.2 if the evaluation lands on yes; no kiosk surface at all if it lands on no) and **B-086** (Bluetooth unlock: 4.1.2, 4.1.3, 1.4.1, and an absolute facility-local expiry rather than a countdown). Both rows already existed and both are blocked on the same partner agreements; what they lacked was criteria, and a criterion written after the vendor spike is a criterion written to fit what got built.
    - **Six findings were merged rather than numbered.** The task-completion form's missing proof fields (UX), its silent success and dropped focus (accessibility) and its placeholder-only labels (UX) are one component and one session — **B-170**. The rate-increase screen's free-text Lease ID and its undistinguished Approve/Cancel pair are one screen — **B-177**. The promotions settings hint and the promotions list's repeated control names are one screen — **B-176**; the waitlist report's `<summary>` names went to **B-183**, the same file as the aging fix, which splits the accessibility review's A5 across three rows because it was three screens. Checkout's unit-lost dead end (UX) and its unspeakable size glyphs (accessibility) are one branch that nobody can reach today, so they are scanned once and fixed once — **B-172**. The lead screen's placeholder labels stayed separate as **B-180** because it is a different screen from the task form, despite arriving in the same finding.
    - **Nothing was rejected as out of scope, and one thing was declined on the reviewer's own advice:** the UX review's "deliberately not raised" list (the sticky price summary, the portal pending-transfer screen, the shared recapture sentence, the banner stack) is left alone, and **B-181 inherits its reviewer's MEDIUM confidence** — it is ordering and disclosure only, and it wants one pass with an operator before the order is committed to.
    - **Five questions are not answered here and are not in `07-decisions.md` yet.** Each is stated in the row that raises it, and **that row cannot be finished until it produces a new D-number**: whether a lien transfer requires re-serving the §59 notice for the new unit (**B-160**); how many days of grace a reversal-re-opened arrear gets, and whether the ladder resumes at the reached stage or restarts (**B-161**); the default ECRI step and cap for a new facility (**B-165**); whether a transfer re-prices to street, carries the in-place rate or takes the greater, and whether a promotion follows the tenant through a transfer — D-89's open half (**B-162**); and whether the accessibility statement's release-blocking sentence is corrected or made true with a deploy gate (**B-159**). They are the owner's calls on legal exposure, tenant retention, revenue and Actions cost respectively; none is answered in PRD prose, and the log gets its new rows when the owner decides, not before.
14. **The ten items added on 2026-08-25 (B-187–B-196), and why the money rows come before the silent ones.** They are the fifth review pass — operator, UX and accessibility over **B-159 through B-184** plus **B-185**, **B-186** and **B-090c** — and the criterion is unchanged from the three blocks before: every one is a defect in built code or an acceptance criterion no row owns, not a new feature. Thirty findings across three reports became ten rows, at `83bwc`–`83bwl`, ahead of the five rows still open (**B-129, B-085, B-086, B-133, B-134**), every one of which is blocked on a partner agreement, an API credential or a real portfolio. **Seven of the ten depend on B-090c, which sits below them at `90e`** — the numbering note above states that exception in full rather than leaving it to be found.
    - **B-187 is first, ahead of four money defects**, for the third time in a row and the same reason: the accessibility statement is a live public claim, CLAUDE.md makes it a standing obligation, and B-184 strengthened one sentence to "everywhere they run" on the strength of a migration that missed five spec files — two of them customer-facing. Unlike B-139 and B-159, this one **makes the sentence true instead of retracting it**, because the gap is five call sites rather than a missing subsystem.
    - **B-188, B-189 and B-190 are the money block, and they come before every silent-failure and comms row in the block.** A payment plan is the one feature here that halts dunning, late fees, access suspension and the lien clock all at once, and none of the three things that decide whether it should is currently checked: progress counts a bounced payment as paid and counts next month's rent as this month's arrears (**B-188**); autopay never hears about the plan and takes the whole balance the night it is agreed while nothing ever collects an installment (**B-189**); and no authority ceiling, no duration cap and no limit on a rolling chain of plans means one manager can defer any amount indefinitely (**B-190**). The order inside the block is causal, not editorial — B-189 and B-190 both read the progress figure B-188 fixes, and building either first would build them against a number that is wrong.
    - **B-191 before B-192 and B-193**, because the comms row settles what a tenant is told and the two UI rows render it. A plan that breaks overnight currently lifts the hold, resumes the ladder and re-arms the gate with **no message of any kind**, while the dashboard card that was the only signal unmounts itself — three reviewers found that independently and it is one row, not three.
    - **B-194 is the only row in the block that is not about payment plans**, and it is here rather than merged because it is one screen and one session: B-186's two notice-given forms discard the server's refusal, announce nothing on success, give the date field a paragraph for an accessible name, and leave two unrelated date fields side by side. Its copy correction is in scope; the charge that copy promises is not, and goes to a D-number as B-186 said it would.
    - **B-196 is deliberately last**, exactly as B-119, B-156 and B-184 were: it asserts what B-191–B-194 fix. It also carries the one finding in the block that is a defect in the *checking* rather than in the product — four hand-checked axe exemptions, each verified once on one element, are matched against every route in the suite, so a real overlap on any page is now suppressed by a note about a badge.
    - **Twenty findings were merged rather than numbered.** The operator's "autopay doesn't know about plans", the UX review's "the plan breaks silently on every surface" and the accessibility review's "there is no plan-broke notice" are **two** rows, not three — the collection mechanism (**B-189**) and the messages (**B-191**) — because they fail for different reasons and one session cannot hold both. The plan builder's twelve unlabelled fields (UX), its unannounced success and dropped focus (accessibility), its unmapped schedule errors (accessibility), its unmarked required reason (UX) and its repeated control names (accessibility) are one component and one session — **B-192**; the D-95 ordering breach was found here rather than reported, and is in the same row because it is the same file. The portal page's single entry point (accessibility), its amount-less pay link (UX) and its missing plan history (UX) are one file — **B-193**. B-186's silent forms (accessibility), its silent success (UX), its two unexplained date fields (UX) and its overpromised charge (UX) are one screen — **B-194**. Five test-coverage gaps became **B-187** (the five unmigrated specs, which is also the public claim) and **B-196** (everything else).
    - **Four findings were declined, and one sub-claim was corrected.** The operator's **tenant-initiated plan requests** is new scope with a leverage argument behind it, not a defect — B-090c scoped it out deliberately and PRD 01 §9 already carries the line; it is written up there as an open gap with its owning questions rather than given a row that would build a policy nobody has set. The operator's **further B-185 test-infra enforcement** is declined on B-185's own evidence: that row proved a general per-suite cleanup is *impossible* while `audit_log`'s trigger refuses `TRUNCATE`, shipped `db:reset-test` as the remedy that actually works, and recorded the residual gap verbatim in `PROGRESS.md`; a new row would restate a known-open note without adding a mechanism. The operator's **down payments and renegotiation** (finding 7) are folded into B-190's D-number rather than built — a plan can already be cancelled and replaced, and what is missing is the rule governing that, not a second feature. The UX review's **"B-181 is now overdue"** is declined as a row: B-181 shipped and D-95 settled the order; what actually broke is that B-090c inserted a section into the read stack in breach of it, which is **B-192**(6). And the UX review's **"the portal plan page has no empty state"** is false on inspection — `portal/payment-plan/page.tsx:37-38` renders one and it is correct; B-193 says so rather than carrying the claim forward.
    - **B-184's T6 is still open and gets no new row.** The three manual passes — VoiceOver over the money path, keyboard over the task queues and the promotions list, the live map — are its own definition of done, they need a person rather than a session, and `PROGRESS.md` already records that none of them happened. Re-raising them as a row would make it look as if a coding session could close them. The rule stands unchanged: a pass not written down did not happen, and the public statement's keyboard sentence stays a disclaimer until one is.
    - **Four questions are not answered here and are appended to `07-decisions.md`** as **D-96** (what payment satisfies a plan installment — any payment, or only payment above the current month's rent), **D-97** (what autopay does for a lease on a plan, in both directions), **D-98** (what a plan may commit to: authority ceiling, duration, plans per lease per year, grace on a missed installment) and **D-99** (whether a short-notice move-out is charged, and on what basis). They are the owner's calls on collections policy, tenant retention, delegated financial authority and legal exposure respectively. **Each is raised in the log as an open row and answered by the owner, not in PRD prose and not by the session that reaches the item** — B-188, B-189, B-190 and B-194 each say so, and none of the four can be finished until its D-number is settled.


## Flagged gaps/conflicts

> **STATUS: ALL RESOLVED 2026-07-30.** Every item below was decided by the owner — see `07-decisions.md` (D-1 through D-11b), which supersedes the text below. The list is kept for historical context only.

1. **Delinquency/lien scope — PRD 02 vs master.** PRD 02 marks the full delinquency, lien, and auction pipeline (US-25–29), POS drawer sessions (US-33), field ops (US-35–37), and all six reports (US-39) as MVP. Master §6 Phase 1 lists only "basic late-fee schedule" and "occupancy & rent-roll report," deferring the full pipeline, drawer, and roll-up depth to Phase 2. Master wins per its own precedence rule; backlog phases follow master (Milestones 5/7 = Phase 2). Owner should confirm.
2. **Marketing Phase 1 scope — PRD 04 vs master.** PRD 04 Phase 1 includes reviews display, review-request emails, promo codes in checkout, lead drips, and abandoned-reservation sequences. Master Phase 1 marketing is only SEO location pages, GBP linkage, and basic lead capture; the rest is master Phase 2. Backlog labels follow master; ordering follows PRD 04 (built together in Milestone 6). Note promotions are also PRD 02 MVP (US-10), so B-070 is kept MVP.
3. **Kiosk mode — master vs PRD 03.** Master Phase 2 hardware includes "kiosk mode (rental flow on an on-site tablet)"; PRD 03 explicitly defers kiosks to Phase 3 with a default answer of "no." Backlog follows PRD 03 (evaluation only, B-085).
4. **First real gate-vendor driver — master vs PRD 03.** Master Phase 2 promises "first real gate integration (one vendor driver)"; PRD 03 Phase 2 ships only stub adapters because partner agreements are out of scope for the learning build. Backlog follows PRD 03 (stubs in B-080; real driver B-085, Phase 3, contingent on a partnership).
5. **Rate increases — PRD 02 MVP vs master Phase 2.** PRD 02 US-11 (scheduled tenant rate increases with notices) is tagged MVP; master puts "rate-change management with tenant notices" in Phase 2. Backlog places it at B-076 (Phase 2).
6. **Stripe Billing vs ledger-driven charges (unresolved joint decision).** PRD 01 OQ-5 recommends admin-ledger-driven PaymentIntents; PRD 05 CN-6 references "Stripe Billing retry config." B-043–B-046 assume ledger-driven per the PRD 01 recommendation — needs an explicit decision before Milestone 4.
7. **Reservation deposit ambiguity.** PRD 01 mandates free, no-card reservations; PRD 02 US-14 describes reservations "with optional deposit"; master OQ-3 leaves the policy open. B-018 builds free-hold only; deposit support is an open owner decision.
8. **Consent store ownership.** PRD 04 defines `contact_consent`; PRD 05 adds `account_sms` and proposes the shared schema own the table (its OQ-8). B-002/B-032/B-072 must implement one shared package — assign ownership at Milestone 1.
9. **PRD cross-reference numbering is inconsistent.** PRD 02's header cites siblings as `01-master`, `03-customer-website`, `04-hardware`, `05-marketing`, but actual filenames are `00-master`, `01-customer-website`, `03-hardware`, `04-marketing`; PRD 05 (communications) is not in the master PRD's four-module feature map (§3) at all. Recommend a doc pass to renumber references and amend master §3 to five modules.
10. **State selection blocks legal configuration.** Master OQ-1 (operating states) gates lien timelines, notice templates, lease clauses, late-fee caps, and SMS disclosure copy (PRD 01 OQ-1, PRD 02 OQ-3, PRD 05 Q2/Q5). Milestone 5 (B-056, B-061) and B-074 cannot ship real defaults without it — needed before Milestone 5 starts.
11. **Minor phasing splits.** Spanish language: PRD 01 Phase 2 vs master Phase 3 multilingual (bundled in B-090). City pages: PRD 04 Phase 2 vs master Phase 3 "per-city/size landing-page generation" (backlog treats city pages as Phase 2 in B-082, generated size-in-city pages as Phase 3 in B-089). Autopay-tenant reminder default (PRD 05 Q1) needs an owner call before B-050 templates.
